| §17.1 | Elementary considerations: critical inverse temperature + antitonicity + characterization + cluster property + summability + susceptibility bound (Steps 141–149) | **Done** | `criticalInverseTemp d J : ENNReal` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassPseudoMassTransfer.lean`): the supremum of all `β ≥ 0` for which `latticeMass d (cubicExhaustion d) ⟨J, 0, β⟩ > 0`. **GJ §17.1 analogy**: Glimm–Jaffe define the critical coupling `σ_c` as the infimum of σ for which the φ⁴ theory has exponential decay; `criticalInverseTemp` is the lattice Ising analog. Properties: `criticalInverseTemp_set_nonempty`; `criticalInverseTemp_nonneg`; `criticalInverseTemp_ge_ofReal_high_temp` (for d≥1, J>0: β_c ≥ 1/(2J·2d)); `criticalInverseTemp_pos` (for d≥1, J>0: β_c > 0). **`criticalInverseTemp_antitone_J`** (Step 142, PR #975): for `0 ≤ J₁ ≤ J₂`, `β_c(J₂) ≤ β_c(J₁)` — stronger coupling → higher T_c → smaller β_c. **Characterization** (Steps 143–145, PRs #976-#978): `criticalInverseTemp_ge_ofReal_of_latticeMass_pos` (mass > 0 at β ≥ 0 → ofReal β ≤ β_c) + `latticeMass_eq_zero_of_criticalInverseTemp_lt` (β_c < ofReal β → mass = 0) + `latticeMass_pos_of_lt_criticalInverseTemp` (ofReal β < β_c and J ≥ 0 → mass > 0; proof: contrapositive using `latticeMass_antitone_beta`). **d = 0 edge case** (Step 145, PR #978): `criticalInverseTemp_eq_top_of_dim_zero` — for d = 0 (single-site, no neighbors), `HasExponentialDecay` is vacuously satisfied at every rate, giving `latticeMass = ⊤` always, hence `criticalInverseTemp 0 J = ⊤` (no phase transition at any temperature). **Cluster property below β_c** (Step 146, PR #979): `HasExponentialDecay_of_latticeMass_pos` (latticeMass > 0 → ∃ α > 0 with HasExponentialDecay; proof via `lt_sSup_iff`) + `clusterProperty_latticeGraph_of_lt_criticalInverseTemp` (ofReal β < β_c and J ≥ 0 → clusterProperty for any exhaustion; proof: β=0 trivial, β>0 uses mass > 0 → positive decay rate → exhaustion transfer via `truncated2Infinite_indep_exhaustion` with `Ferromagnetic` → `clusterProperty_latticeGraph_of_HasExponentialDecay`). **GJ §17.1 Thm 17.1.1 Ising analog** (correlations monotone in h and β): already proved as `correlationInfinite_monotone_h` / `correlationInfinite_monotone_beta` (GKS-II). **Summability below β_c** (Step 147, PR #980): `truncated2Infinite_summable_of_lt_criticalInverseTemp` — for J ≥ 0, β ≥ 0, β < β_c: `Summable (j ↦ truncated2Infinite (latticeGraph d) Λ ⟨J,0,β⟩ i j)`. Extends `truncated2Infinite_summable_of_high_temp` (PR #903, βJD < 1) to the full high-temperature regime. Proof: β=0 trivially summable; β>0: extract HasExponentialDecay rate α > 0 → `|U_2(i,j)| ≤ C·exp(-α·d(i,j))` for j≠i and `U_2(i,i)=0` (Z₂) → `summable_exp_neg_dist` + `Summable.of_nonneg_of_le`. **GJ §17.1 Cor 17.1.2 Ising analog** (magnetization monotone in β): `magnetizationInfinite_monotone_beta`; β_c antitone in J: `criticalInverseTemp_antitone_J`. **Susceptibility bound below β_c** (Step 148, PR #982): `susceptibilityInfinite_le_tsum_truncated2Infinite` (in `Inequalities/HighTemp.lean`) — for ferromagnetic ⟨J,0,β⟩ and Summable truncated2Infinite, `susceptibilityInfinite ≤ ∑' j, truncated2Infinite i j`. Proof: `ciSup_le`, then per-stage bound via `susceptibility_h_zero` + `correlationAlongExhaustion ≤ correlationInfinite` chain + `Finset.sum_coe_sort` + `Summable.sum_le_tsum`. Combined with Step 147: β < β_c and J ≥ 0 imply finite susceptibility. **Finite susceptibility below β_c** (Step 149, PR #983): `susceptibilityInfinite_latticeGraph_le_tsum_of_lt_criticalInverseTemp` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassLebowitzDerivative.lean`, moved from `Inequalities.lean` in PR #1928) — for J ≥ 0, β ≥ 0, β < β_c: `susceptibilityInfinite (latticeGraph d) Λ ⟨J,0,β⟩ i ≤ ∑' j, truncated2Infinite ... i j`. β=0 case: both sides 0 (truncated2 vanishes); β>0: apply Step 148 + Step 147. **J = 0 special case** (Step 151, PR #985): `criticalInverseTemp_eq_top_of_J_zero`: for any d, criticalInverseTemp d 0 = ⊤ — with no coupling, `latticeMass = ⊤` at every β, the defining set is [0,∞), and sSup = ⊤. Proof: `ENNReal.iSup_natCast` + per-n `latticeMass_top_of_{beta_zero, J_zero}`. (PRs #974-#983, #985, Steps 141–151.) |

| §17.3 | **Thm 17.3.1 / Thm 17.3.2 theorem body** (coupling-constant `λ_phys` bound, pp. 307–308) — scope determination | **Out of scope** | This row concerns the **theorem bodies** Thm 17.3.1 and Thm 17.3.2 (bounds on the physical coupling constant `λ_phys`), *not* the auxiliary inequality labelled eq. (17.3.1) — the latter's lattice Ising analog (two-sided bound on `U₄^∞`) is separately **Done** in the "GJ eq. (17.3.1) auxiliary `U₄` two-sided bound" row below; the same numeral `17.3.1` names two different objects. GJ Theorems 17.3.1 and 17.3.2 concern the coupling constant `λ_phys = χ^{−4}·m^{d−4}·∫⟨φ(x₁)···φ(x₄)⟩_T dx₁dx₂dx₃dx₄`. The definition and bounds require: (i) the continuum Lehmann spectral measure `ρ(a)` and its integral representation `χ = ∫ dρ(a)/a`; (ii) position-space integrals `∫...dx₁dx₂dx₃dx₄` over ℝ^d; (iii) continuum Green's-function decay estimates from `(−Δ+a)^{−1}(x,y)`. Additionally, GJ's own proof of Thm 17.3.1 is only an outline (p. 307: "For details, see the original paper"), with no complete self-contained proof in the book. Unlike Thm 17.2.1 (which explicitly states "or Ising model" on p. 305), §17.3's setup is stated entirely for continuum φ⁴ field theory. This classical lattice Gibbs-measure project does not build continuum Lehmann/Green's-function/position-space-integral machinery; formalization is blocked on machine-checked proof of an un-cited external result (GJ's omitted steps) combined with missing continuum infrastructure. GJ pp. 307–308, and separate continuum QFT apparatus not in scope. Issue #4405 group 2c. |
| §17.4 | Particle existence and mass derivative (Thm 17.4.1 / Thm 17.4.2, p. 309) — scope determination | **Out of scope** | GJ Theorems 17.4.1 and 17.4.2 concern particle existence (for almost every m, Z(σ) → 0) and the mass derivative bound `dm²/dσ ≤ Z` relating the field-strength renormalization constant Z to the mass. The proofs are entirely in momentum-space Fourier formalism: the two-point structure `S(p) = Z/(p²+m²) + ∫ dρ(a)/(m²+a+p²)` (Källén–Lehmann spectral representation with continuum spectral measure `dρ(a)`), differential-geometric analysis of the "one-particle curve" `p² = −m²(σ)` in Fourier space, and bounds on the Euclidean momentum `p`. No lattice/combinatorial derivation is presented in the book. §17.4 assumes a Hamiltonian on Hilbert space H with spectral machinery that this classical lattice Gibbs-measure project deliberately does not build. GJ p. 309, and continuum momentum-space QFT apparatus entirely out of scope. Issue #4405 group 2d. |
| §17.5 | Correlation length | **Done (high-temperature regime): latticeMass > 0 + antitonicity + exhaustion-independence** | `IsingModel.Ambient.HasExponentialDecay d Λ p α` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassFoundation.lean`): `∃ C ≥ 0, ∀ i ≠ j, |U_2(i, j)| ≤ C · exp(-α · latticeDistance d i j)`. Trivial slices: `HasExponentialDecay_beta_zero` / `HasExponentialDecay_J_zero`. Sanity: `HasExponentialDecay_mono`. **Linkage** `clusterProperty_latticeGraph_of_HasExponentialDecay`: α > 0 → clusterProperty. **Lattice mass** `latticeMass d Λ p : ENNReal := sSup ((↑·) '' { α : NNReal | HasExponentialDecay d Λ p α })`; `latticeMass_ge_of_HasExponentialDecay` turns any validating nonnegative decay rate into `ofReal α ≤ latticeMass`, and `latticeMass_pos_of_HasExponentialDecay` turns a positive validating rate into `0 < latticeMass`. Trivial-slice tops: `latticeMass_top_of_beta_zero`, `latticeMass_top_of_J_zero`. **High-temperature positive mass (Step 111, PR #917; moved to `LatticeMassHighTemperature.lean` in PR #1926)**: `latticeMass_pos_of_high_temp`: for `0 < βJ` and `βJD < 1` (D=2d), `0 < latticeMass d (cubicExhaustion d) ⟨J,0,β⟩`. Proof: d=0 case vacuous; d≥1: rate α₀ = -log(βJD) > 0 from Step 110, so `latticeMass ≥ α₀ > 0`. **Antitonicity (Step 112, PR #919; moved to `LatticeMassHighTemperature.lean` in PR #1926)**: `latticeMass_antitone_beta`: for `0 < β₁ ≤ β₂` and `J ≥ 0`, `latticeMass(β₂) ≤ latticeMass(β₁)` (higher temperature → larger mass). `latticeMass_antitone_J`: for `0 ≤ J₁ ≤ J₂` and `β > 0`, `latticeMass(J₂) ≤ latticeMass(J₁)`. Both via GKS-II β/J-monotonicity of correlation + truncated2 = correlation at h=0. (PRs #793-#795, #917, #919.) **Exhaustion-independence (Step 150, PR #984)**: `latticeMass_indep_exhaustion`: for ferromagnetic `p`, `latticeMass d Λ p = latticeMass d Λ' p` for any two exhaustions `Λ, Λ'`. Proof: `truncated2Infinite_indep_exhaustion` → set equality → sSup equality. Corollary: `latticeMass_indep_cubicExhaustion`. **Explicit lower bound (Step 152, PR #986; moved to `LatticeMassHighTemperature.lean` in PR #1926)**: `latticeMass_ge_neg_log_of_high_temp` — for d ≥ 1, 0 < βJ, βJ·2d < 1: `ofReal(-log(βJ·2d)) ≤ latticeMass`. Makes the implicit lower bound from `latticeMass_pos_of_high_temp` (Step 111) explicit as a quantitative bound. **Two-sided bound (Step 153, PR #987; moved to `LatticeMassHighTemperature.lean` in PR #1926)**: `latticeMass_two_sided_bound` — bundles Steps 115+152: `ofReal(-log(βJ·2d)) ≤ latticeMass ≤ ofReal(-log(tanh(βJ)))` for d ≥ 1, J > 0, β > 0, βJ·2d < 1. **J-lower bound on two-point function (Step 113; moved to `LatticeMassHighTemperature.lean` in PR #1926)**: `twoPointFunction_ge_tanh_betaJ_of_adj`: for adjacent `r` in `latticeGraph d`, ferromagnetic `J ≥ 0`, `β > 0`, `h = 0`: `tanh(βJ) ≤ twoPointFunction d ⟨J,0,β⟩ r`. Proof: single-edge subgraph G_single ≤ latticeGraph d; correlationInfinite G_single = tanh(βJ) by 2-site computation; GKS-II subgraph monotonicity. **Path lower bound (Step 114, PR #921; moved to `LatticeMassHighTemperature.lean` in PR #1926)**: `twoPointFunction_ge_tanh_betaJ_pow_dist`: for `r ≠ 0`, ferromagnetic `J ≥ 0`, `β > 0`, `h = 0`: `tanh(βJ)^(latticeDistance d 0 r) ≤ twoPointFunction d ⟨J,0,β⟩ r`. Proof: strong induction on n = latticeDistance d 0 r; `exists_latticeDistance_succ_adj` gives geodesic predecessor v; Step 113 handles adjacent case; for n ≥ 1 use GKS-II product inequality (`correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_cubicExhaustion_gks_second`) via {0,v} ∆ {v,r} = {0,r} with translation invariance of correlationInfinite. **Upper bound on lattice mass (Step 115, PR #923; moved to `LatticeMassHighTemperature.lean` in PR #1926)**: `latticeMass_le_neg_log_tanh_betaJ`: for `d ≥ 1`, `J > 0`, `β > 0`, `h = 0`: `latticeMass d (cubicExhaustion d) ⟨J,0,β⟩ ≤ ENNReal.ofReal (-log(tanh(βJ)))`. Combined with Step 111 lower bound, gives two-sided bound `-log(βJD) ≤ latticeMass ≤ -log(tanh(βJ))`. Proof: contrapositive — if `HasExponentialDecay` at rate `α > -log(tanh(βJ))`, take axis point `r_n = (n₀,0,...,0)` with `dist(0, r_n) = n₀` (`latticeDistance_coord_eq`); Step 114 gives `tanh(βJ)^n₀ ≤ twoPointFunction d p r_n ≤ C·exp(-α·n₀)`, so `exp(ε·n₀) ≤ C` where `ε = log(tanh(βJ))+α > 0`; Archimedean gives n₀ with `ε·n₀ > C`, contradicting `exp(ε·n₀) ≥ ε·n₀+1 > C`. |

(Steps 167–169 now live in `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassHighTempLipschitz.lean`; moved from `Inequalities.lean` in PR #1929.) **Step 170 (uniform convergence of corr_n → corr_∞)**: `correlationAlongExhaustion_tendstoUniformlyOn_beta`: for any exhaustion Λ, vertices r_val ≠ s_val, `0 ≤ J`, `0 < a ≤ b`, `bJ·2d < 1`: the finite-volume two-point functions converge uniformly on `[a,b]` to the infinite-volume limit. Proof: Dini's theorem (`Monotone.tendstoUniformlyOn_of_forall_tendsto`) with (1) continuity of each corr_n (from `correlation_continuousAt_beta`); (2) monotonicity in n (from `correlationAlongExhaustion_monotone`); (3) continuity of corr_∞ (Step 169); (4) pointwise convergence (`correlationAlongExhaustion_tendsto_ciSup`). (Step 170 now lives in `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassHighTempLipschitz.lean`; moved from `Inequalities.lean` in PR #1929.) **Step 171 (a.e. differentiability of corr_∞ in β)**: `correlationInfinite_ae_differentiableWithinAt_beta_of_high_temp`: for any exhaustion Λ, vertices r_val ≠ s_val, `0 ≤ J`, `0 < a ≤ b`, `bJ·2d < 1`: `β ↦ corr_∞(β)` is differentiable within `[a,b]` at Lebesgue-a.e. β. Proof: analytic corollary of Step 168 (Lipschitz) via Rademacher's theorem (`LipschitzOnWith.ae_differentiableWithinAt_real`). Note: this is a preparatory corollary of the §17.5 Lipschitz program; everywhere-differentiability of corr_∞ in β (which is the content of the GJ §17.5–17.6 derivative analysis) requires additional work on uniform convergence of derivative sequences. (Step 171, PR #1000.) **Step 172 (a.e. differentiability on open high-temperature interval)**: `correlationInfinite_locallyBoundedVariationOn_beta_of_high_temp` + `correlationInfinite_ae_differentiableWithinAt_beta_of_high_temp_open`: for `0 < J`, `1 ≤ d`: `β ↦ corr_∞(β)` is locally bounded variation on `Ioo 0 (1/(J·2d))` (the open high-temperature region) and a.e. differentiable within it. Proof: covering by closed intervals `[a, b] ⊂ Ioo 0 β_c` where Step 168 gives Lipschitz; then `LocallyBoundedVariationOn.ae_differentiableWithinAt`. Strengthens Step 171 from a fixed closed interval to the entire open interval `(0, β_c)`. (Step 172, PR #1002.) **Step 173 (continuity on open high-temperature interval)**: `correlationInfinite_continuousOn_beta_of_high_temp_open`: for `0 < J`, `1 ≤ d`: `β ↦ corr_∞(β)` is continuous on the open interval `Ioo 0 (1/(J·2d))`. Proof: for each β₀ in the interval, choose `[a, b] ⊂ Ioo 0 β_c` with `β₀ ∈ Ioo a b` (e.g., `a = β₀/2`, `b = (β₀+β_c)/2`); Step 169 gives continuity on `[a, b]`, hence at β₀. Strengthens Step 169 from a fixed closed interval to the entire open interval. (Step 173, PR #1003.) **Step 174 (locally uniform convergence on open high-temperature interval)**: `correlationAlongExhaustion_tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_beta_of_high_temp_open`: for `0 < J`, `1 ≤ d`: corr_n → corr_∞ locally uniformly on `Ioo 0 (1/(J·2d))`. Proof: `Monotone.tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_of_forall_tendsto` (Mathlib Dini, locally-uniform variant) using (1) ContinuousOn of each corr_n; (2) Monotonicity in n via `correlationAlongExhaustion_monotone`; (3) ContinuousOn of corr_∞ (Step 173); (4) pointwise convergence. Strengthens Step 170 from compact `[a, b]` to locally uniform on `Ioo 0 β_c`. (Step 174, PR #1004.) **Step 175 (ContinuousAt at every interior point)**: `correlationInfinite_continuousAt_beta_of_high_temp`: for any β₀ ∈ Ioo 0 (1/(J·2d)): corr_∞ is `ContinuousAt` at β₀ (full neighborhood, not just within-set). Proof: open set is a neighborhood of every interior point ⇒ `ContinuousOn.continuousAt`. Useful when callers need full continuity rather than within-restriction. (Step 175, PR #1006; Steps 171–175 now live in `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassHighTempLipschitz.lean`, moved from `Inequalities.lean` in PR #1929.) **Step 176 (linear bound at β = 0; right-continuity at 0)**: `correlationInfinite_le_const_mul_beta_of_high_temp` (private helper `inducedLatticeGraph_correlation_le_const_mul_beta`): for `0 ≤ J`, `0 < b`, `bJ·2d < 1`, `β ∈ (0, b]`, `r ≠ s`: `corr_∞(r, s, β) ≤ (J·M² + J·4d) · β` where `M = bJ·2d/(1 - bJ·2d)`. Proof: per-stage: by uniform-in-n Lipschitz (Step 167) on `[a, b]` and monotonicity, `corr_n(β) ≤ corr_n(a) + C·(β−a)` for any `0 < a ≤ β`; take `a → 0⁺` using continuity of corr_n at 0 with corr_n(0) = 0 (`correlation_beta_zero_vanish_of_nonempty_A`) via `ge_of_tendsto` on `nhdsWithin 0 (Ioi 0)`. Then `ciSup_le` over n. As an immediate corollary, `corr_∞(r, s, β) → 0` as β → 0⁺ (right-continuity at 0). (Step 176, PR #1007.) **Step 177 (ContinuousOn on closed interval [0, b] including β = 0)**: `correlationInfinite_continuousOn_beta_of_high_temp_zero_closed` (helper `correlationInfinite_eq_zero_at_beta_zero`): for `0 < J`, `1 ≤ d`, `0 < b`, `bJ·2d < 1`: `β ↦ corr_∞(β)` continuous on `[0, b]`. Proof: at β > 0 use Step 175 (`continuousAt`); at β = 0 use Step 176 squeeze `0 ≤ corr_∞(x) ≤ C·x` to construct ε–δ via `Metric.tendsto_nhdsWithin_nhds`. Strengthens Step 169 from `[a, b]` (a > 0) to closed `[0, b]`. (Step 177, PR #1010.) **Step 178 (TendstoUniformlyOn on [0, b] including β = 0)**: `correlationAlongExhaustion_tendstoUniformlyOn_beta_zero_closed` (helper `correlationAlongExhaustion_eq_zero_at_beta_zero`): for `0 < J`, `1 ≤ d`, `0 < b`, `bJ·2d < 1`: corr_n → corr_∞ uniformly on `[0, b]`. Proof: Dini's theorem (`Monotone.tendstoUniformlyOn_of_forall_tendsto`) on the compact `[0, b]` with: (1) ContinuousOn each corr_n; (2) Monotonicity in n at β = 0 trivial (both sides 0), at β > 0 from `correlationAlongExhaustion_monotone`; (3) ContinuousOn corr_∞ from Step 177; (4) pointwise convergence at β = 0 trivial, at β > 0 from `correlationAlongExhaustion_tendsto_ciSup`. Strengthens Step 170 from `[a, b]` (a > 0) to closed `[0, b]`. (Step 178, PR #1011.) **Step 179 (a.e. differentiable on closed [0, b] including β = 0)**: `correlationInfinite_monotoneOn_beta_zero_closed` + `correlationInfinite_ae_differentiableWithinAt_beta_zero_closed`: for `0 ≤ J`: `β ↦ corr_∞(β)` is monotone non-decreasing on `[0, b]` and a.e. differentiable within `[0, b]`. Proof: monotonicity at β > 0 from `correlationInfinite_monotone_beta`, at β = 0 from `correlationInfinite_nonneg`. Then `MonotoneOn.locallyBoundedVariationOn` (without high-temperature hypothesis!) + `LocallyBoundedVariationOn.ae_differentiableWithinAt`. (Step 179, PR #1012.) **Step 180 (LipschitzOnWith on closed [0, b] including β = 0)**: `correlationInfinite_lipschitzOnWith_beta_zero_closed` (helper `correlationInfinite_diff_le_const_mul_diff`): for `0 ≤ J`, `0 < b`, `bJ·2d < 1`: `β ↦ corr_∞(β)` is `C`-Lipschitz on `[0, b]` with the same constant `C = J·M² + J·4d` as Step 168. Proof: helper combines Step 168 (β₁ > 0 case) with Step 176 linear bound (β₁ = 0 case); wrapper uses `LipschitzOnWith.of_dist_le_mul` with `abs_sub_comm` to flip signs and `MonotoneOn` (Step 179) to drop absolute values. Strengthens Step 168 from `[a, b]` (a > 0) to closed `[0, b]`. (Step 180, PR #1014.) **Step 181 (linear bound at β = 0 extended to β ≥ 0)**: `correlationInfinite_le_const_mul_beta_of_high_temp_zero_incl`: linear bound from Step 176 extended to β ∈ [0, b] (where β = 0 case is `corr_∞(0) = 0 ≤ C·0` trivially). Useful as a unified bound when β = 0 is allowed. (Step 181, PR #1015.) **Step 182 (ContinuousOn on Ico 0 β_c, half-open)**: `correlationInfinite_continuousOn_beta_of_high_temp_Ico`: combines Step 173 (Ioo 0 β_c) with Step 177 (Icc 0 b for any b < β_c) to give continuity on `Ico 0 (1/(J·2d))` (closed at 0, open at β_c). For β₀ = 0 use Step 177 with b = β_c/2 + `mono_of_mem_nhdsWithin`; for β₀ > 0 use Step 175. (Step 182, PR #1016.) **Step 183 (MonotoneOn + ae differentiable on Ici 0)**: `correlationInfinite_monotoneOn_beta_Ici_zero` + `correlationInfinite_ae_differentiableWithinAt_beta_Ici_zero`: corr_∞ is monotone non-decreasing on the entire `Ici 0` and a.e. differentiable. Proof: β = 0 case from `correlationInfinite_eq_zero_at_beta_zero` + nonnegativity; β > 0 case from existing `correlationInfinite_monotone_beta` (Ioi 0). Then `MonotoneOn.locallyBoundedVariationOn` + `ae_differentiableWithinAt`. No high-temperature condition. (Step 183, PR #1017.) **Step 184 (TendstoLocallyUniformlyOn on Ico 0 β_c)**: `correlationAlongExhaustion_tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_beta_Ico`: corr_n → corr_∞ locally uniformly on the half-open `Ico 0 (1/(J·2d))`. Proof: `Monotone.tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_of_forall_tendsto` with (1) ContinuousOn each corr_n; (2) monotonicity in n (β = 0 trivial); (3) ContinuousOn corr_∞ (Step 182); (4) pointwise convergence. Strengthens Step 174 from Ioo 0 β_c to Ico 0 β_c. (Step 186, PR #1020; module split to `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassTruncated2HighTemp.lean`, moved from `Inequalities.lean`, PR #1931.) **Step 187 (truncated2Infinite MonotoneOn β on Ici 0 at h = 0)**: `truncated2Infinite_monotoneOn_beta_Ici_zero`: monotone non-decreasing on Ici 0. (Step 187, PR #1021; module split to `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassTruncated2HighTemp.lean`, moved from `Inequalities.lean`, PR #1931.) **Step 188 (finite-volume susceptibility ContinuousAt β at h = 0)**: `truncated2_continuousAt_beta` (in `BetaDerivative.lean`) + `susceptibility_continuousAt_beta` (in `PhaseTransition.lean`). At h = 0, `truncated2 = correlation - correlation·correlation` is continuous in β; the susceptibility `χ(i, β) = ∑_j truncated2(i, j, β)` is then continuous via `tendsto_finset_sum`. Finite-volume only — infinite-volume susceptibility continuity requires uniform convergence over the infinite sum (open). (Step 213, PR #1048; retirement PR #4839.) **Step 214 (J-derivative explicit formula)**: `hasDerivAt_correlation_J` (`JDerivative.lean`): `d/dJ ⟨σ^A⟩ = β · Σ_{e∈E} [⟨σ^{A△{e₁,e₂}}⟩ − ⟨σ^A⟩·⟨σ_{e₁e₂}⟩]` at general `h`. Building blocks: `hasDerivAt_boltzmannWeight_J`, `hasDerivAt_partitionFunction_J`, `hasDerivAt_gibbsExpectation_J` (private). Mirrors Step 117a (β-derivative) but holds at any h since the magnetic-field term in the Hamiltonian is independent of J. Proof via chain rule on `exp(β·J·Σ_e edgeSpin σ e + C)`, quotient rule on Z⁻¹·∑F·bw, and `spinProduct_mul`. (Step 214, PR #1049.) **Step 215 (truncated2 + magnetization + susceptibility HasDerivAt J)**: `truncated2_hasDerivAt_J` (`JDerivative.lean`) + `magnetization_hasDerivAt_J` + `susceptibility_hasDerivAt_J` (`PhaseTransition.lean`). truncated2 J-derivative via product rule on `correlation {i,j} - correlation {i}·correlation {j}`; magnetization J-derivative directly from `hasDerivAt_correlation_J` at A = {i}; susceptibility J-derivative `∑_j deriv (truncated2 i j)` via `HasDerivAt.fun_sum`. Holds at any h. Parallels Steps 191/197/198 in β direction. (Step 215, PR #1050.) **Step 216 (J-derivative Lebowitz bound)**: `correlation_J_deriv_le_lebowitz` (`JDerivative.lean`): `d/dJ ⟨σ_r σ_s⟩|_{h=0} ≤ β · Σ_e [corr{r,e₁}·corr{s,e₂} + corr{r,e₂}·corr{s,e₁}] + β · |E(G)|`. Direct J-direction analogue of Step 117b (`correlation_beta_deriv_le_lebowitz`). Same proof structure with prefactor `J → β`, using `hasDerivAt_correlation_J` and the public-now `summand_le_one` helper. (Step 216, PR #1051.) **Step 217 (tight J-derivative Lebowitz bound)**: `correlation_J_deriv_le_lebowitz_tight` (`JDerivative.lean`): improves Step 216 with error term `β·|{e: r∈e ∨ s∈e}|` (incident edges only) instead of `β·|E|`. Direct J-direction analogue of Step 154 (`correlation_beta_deriv_le_lebowitz_tight`). Same proof structure with prefactor `J → β`. (Step 217, PR #1052.) **Step 218 (J-derivative bound on ℤ^d)**: `inducedLatticeGraph_J_deriv_le` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassLebowitzDerivative.lean`): for any Λ ⊆ ℤ^d and r≠s in ↑Λ, `d/dJ corr{r,s}|_{h=0} ≤ β·Σ_e Lebowitz_e + β·4d`. Combines Step 217 (tight bound) with Step 155 (incident edges ≤ 4d) via `linarith`. Direct J-direction analogue of Step 157. (Step 218, PR #1053.) **Step 219 (J-derivative bound by χ_∞²)**: `inducedLatticeGraph_J_deriv_le_susc_sq`: under `BddAbove` of susceptibilities, `d/dJ corr_n(r,s)|_{h=0} ≤ β·χ_∞(r)·χ_∞(s) + β·4d`. Combines Step 218 with Step 162 (`Σ_leb ≤ χ_∞²` under BddAbove). Direct J-direction analogue of Step 163. (Step 219, PR #1054.) **Step 220 (unconditional J-deriv bound under high-temp)**: `inducedLatticeGraph_J_deriv_le_susc_sq_high_temp`: under `0 ≤ J`, `0 < β`, `βJ·2d < 1`, `d/dJ corr_n(r,s)|_{h=0} ≤ β·χ_∞²·... + β·4d`, no BddAbove hypothesis. Step 164 supplies BddAbove via high-temp; then Step 219 closes. Direct J-direction analogue of Step 166. (Step 220, PR #1055.) **Step 221 (uniform J-Lipschitz for finite-volume corr_n)**: `inducedLatticeGraph_correlation_norm_sub_le_J` (helper): for any stage `n` and J₁, J₂ ∈ [a, b] with `0 < a ≤ b`, `bβ·2d < 1`: `‖corr_n(J₂) - corr_n(J₁)‖ ≤ (β·M² + β·4d)·‖J₂-J₁‖` where `M = bβ·2d/(1-bβ·2d)`. Direct J-direction analogue of Step 167. Proof: MVT (`Convex.norm_image_sub_le_of_norm_deriv_le`); derivative bounded by (a) non-negativity from `correlation_monotone_J` + `HasDerivWithinAt.nonneg_of_monotoneOn`; (b) upper bound via Step 220 + `susceptibilityInfinite_latticeGraph_le_of_high_temp_gen` + monotonicity of x/(1-x). (Step 221, PR #1056.) **Step 222 (Lipschitz of corr_∞ in J on [a, b])**: `correlationInfinite_lipschitzOnWith_J_of_high_temp`: for any exhaustion Λ, vertices r_val ≠ s_val, `0 < β`, `0 < a ≤ b`, `bβ·2d < 1`: `J ↦ correlationInfinite ... ⟨J,0,β⟩ {r_val,s_val}` is `C`-Lipschitz on `[a,b]` with `C = β·M² + β·4d`, `M = bβ·2d/(1-bβ·2d)`. Direct J-direction analogue of Step 168. Proof: `LipschitzOnWith.of_dist_le_mul`; for J₁ ≤ J₂, monotonicity of corr_∞ in J (`correlationInfinite_monotone_J`) gives sign; for each stage `n`: Step 221 + `le_ciSup` give `corr_n(J₂) ≤ corr_∞(J₁) + C·(J₂-J₁)`; `ciSup_le` closes. (Step 222, PR #1057.) **Step 223 (continuity of corr_∞ in J)**: `correlationInfinite_continuousOn_J_of_high_temp`: `J ↦ correlationInfinite ... {r_val,s_val}` is continuous on `[a,b]`. Direct J-direction analogue of Step 169. Trivial wrapper: Lipschitz → continuous. (Step 223, PR #1058.) **Step 224 (uniform convergence of corr_n → corr_∞ in J)**: `correlationAlongExhaustion_tendstoUniformlyOn_J`: for `0 < β`, `0 < a ≤ b`, `bβ·2d < 1`: corr_n → corr_∞ uniformly on `[a, b]` in J at h = 0. Direct J-direction analogue of Step 170. Proof: Dini's theorem (`Monotone.tendstoUniformlyOn_of_forall_tendsto`) with (1) ContinuousOn each corr_n; (2) Monotonicity in n; (3) ContinuousOn corr_∞ (Step 223); (4) pointwise convergence. (Step 224, PR #1059.) **Step 225 (a.e. differentiability of corr_∞ in J)**: `correlationInfinite_ae_differentiableWithinAt_J_of_high_temp`: `J ↦ corr_∞(J)` differentiable within `[a, b]` at Lebesgue-a.e. J. Direct J-direction analogue of Step 171. Trivial wrapper: Step 222 + Rademacher (`LipschitzOnWith.ae_differentiableWithinAt_real`). Direct J-direction analogue of Step 172. Strengthens Step 225 from a fixed `[a, b]` to the entire open interval `(0, J_c)`. (Step 226, PR #1061.) **Step 227 (continuity on Ioo 0 J_c)**: `correlationInfinite_continuousOn_J_of_high_temp_open`: for `0 < β`, `1 ≤ d`: `J ↦ corr_∞(J)` is continuous on `Ioo 0 (1/(β·2d))`. Direct J-direction analogue of Step 173. For each J₀ in the interval, pick `[a, b]` with J₀ in interior, apply Step 223. (Step 227, PR #1062.) **Step 228 (locally uniform on Ioo 0 J_c)**: `correlationAlongExhaustion_tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_J_of_high_temp_open`: for `0 < β`, `1 ≤ d`: corr_n → corr_∞ locally uniformly on `Ioo 0 (1/(β·2d))` in J. Direct J-direction analogue of Step 174. Proof: Mathlib Dini locally-uniform variant + Step 227 + monotonicity in n + pointwise convergence. (Step 228, PR #1063.) **Step 229 (ContinuousAt every J ∈ Ioo 0 J_c)**: `correlationInfinite_continuousAt_J_of_high_temp`: for any `J₀ ∈ Ioo 0 (1/(β·2d))`: corr_∞ is `ContinuousAt` at J₀ (full neighborhood). (Step 242, PR #1077.) **Step 243 (general-h β-derivative formula)**: `hasDerivAt_correlation_beta_general_h` (`BetaDerivative.lean`): `d/dβ ⟨σ^A⟩ = J·Σ_e [⟨σ^{A△e}⟩ - ⟨σ^A⟩·⟨σ^e⟩] + h·Σ_i [⟨σ^{A△{i}}⟩ - ⟨σ^A⟩·⟨σ_i⟩]` at any (J, h, β). Extends Step 117a (`hasDerivAt_correlation_beta`) which is restricted to h = 0 by adding the h-term. Proof: quotient rule + new helper `neg_hamiltonian_decomp_general` (-H = J·Σedge + h·Σ_i spinProduct{i}); decompose ⟨F·(-H)⟩ and ⟨-H⟩ over both sums; use `spinProduct_mul` for both edge and singleton terms. The second sum equals `h·χ(i)` (susceptibility) at h=0 it's the Step 198 derivative; at general h it's the magnetization β-derivative. At J = 0 sites are non-interacting; off-diagonal truncated correlation factorizes to zero (Z₂ symmetry). Diagonal value picks up the `{i, i} = {i}` Finset collapse asymmetry. Proof: each correlation factorizes as `tanh(βh)^|A|`; substituting gives `t³ - 3t·t² + 2t³ = 0`. (Step 276, PR #1112.) **Step 277 (truncated4Infinite at J = 0 distinct sites)**: `truncated4Infinite_J_zero`: for pairwise distinct `i, j, k, l`, `U_4(i, j, k, l) = -2·tanh(βh)^4` at J = 0. Note ≤ 0, consistent with Cor 4.3.3 (Lebowitz inequality at h = 0, where this gives 0). Proof: `correlationInfinite_J_zero` factorization gives `t^4 - 3·t^2·t^2 = -2t^4`. (Step 277, PR #1113.) **Step 278 (cleanup: remove duplicate truncated J = 0 theorems from Steps 275-277)**: Removed `truncated2Infinite_J_zero_off_diag` / `_diag` / `truncated3Infinite_J_zero` / `truncated4Infinite_J_zero` (added in Steps 275-277) since they duplicated pre-existing `truncated2Infinite_J_zero_of_ne` / `_diagonal` / `truncated3Infinite_J_zero_of_pairwise_distinct` / `truncated4Infinite_J_zero_of_pairwise_distinct`. (Step 278, PR #1115.) **Step 279 (correlationInfinite at J = h = 0 vanishes for nonempty A)**: `correlationInfinite_zero_params_vanish_of_nonempty_A` (`AmbientLattice/MagnetizationInfinite.lean`): at J = h = 0, β > 0, ferromagnetic, for nonempty A: `correlationInfinite ⟨0, 0, β⟩ A = 0`. Specialization of `correlationInfinite_J_zero` at h = 0 (tanh(0) = 0, 0^|A| = 0 for |A| ≥ 1). (Step 279, PR #1116.) **Step 280 (ℤ^d correlationInfinite zero_params)**: `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_zero_params_vanish_of_nonempty_A` (`UniformMag.lean`). ℤ^d wrapper for Step 279. (Step 280, PR #1117.) |
| §17.5 | Exponential-decay exhaustion transfer | **Done** | `HasExponentialDecay_transfer_exhaustion` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassPseudoMassTransfer.lean`): under ferromagnetic parameters, any validating exponential-decay rate for one exhaustion transfers to any other exhaustion. Proof: rewrite the truncated 2-point function by `truncated2Infinite_indep_exhaustion` and reuse the same constant and rate. This is the reusable transfer step used by the latticeMass/cluster-property bridge and by later Step 117l arguments. |
| §17.5 | Uniform high-temperature exponential decay across exhaustions | **Done (high-temperature rate)** | `HasExponentialDecay_transfer_high_temp` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassPseudoMassTransferExpDecay.lean`), `latticeMass_ge_neg_log_of_high_temp_exhaustion`, and `latticeMass_pos_of_high_temp_exhaustion` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassPseudoMassTransferExpDecayMass.lean`): the Simon--Lieb high-temperature rate `-log(βJ·2d)`, originally proved for `cubicExhaustion`, transfers to every exhaustion via `truncated2Infinite_indep_exhaustion`; consequently every exhaustion has the same explicit lower bound on `latticeMass`, and every positive-dimensional exhaustion (`d ≥ 1`) has positive mass in the strict high-temperature regime. | §17.5 | Pseudo-mass profile function (Step 117c) | **Done** | `pseudoMassG α r t := 2·exp(-(t·r))/(1+(t·r)^α)` (`PseudoMass.lean`). Properties: `pseudoMassG_zero` (g(0)=2), `pseudoMassG_pos` (g>0 for t≥0,r>0), `pseudoMassG_le_two`, `pseudoMassG_strictAntiOn` (strict antitonicity on [0,∞)), `pseudoMassG_continuousOn`, `pseudoMassG_tendsto_zero` (g→0 as t→∞ via squeeze). Existence: `pseudoMassG_exists_of_mem_Ioo` (c∈(0,2) → ∃t≥0, g(t)=c, by IVT). Uniqueness: `pseudoMassG_unique` (by strict antitonicity). Note: α is a natural-number specialization of GJ's general α>d/2. (PR #926 Step 117c.) |
| §17.5 | Pseudo-mass derivative formula + definition (Step 117d) | **Done** | `pseudoMassG_hasDerivAt (α : ℕ)`: HasDerivAt formula via quotient rule (no hα required). `pseudoMassG_deriv_neg`: derivative < 0 for t>0, r>0. `pseudoMass`: Classical.choose definition (unique t≥0 with g(t)=c). `pseudoMass_spec/nonneg/eq_iff`. (PR #926 Step 117d.) |
| §17.5 | Pseudo-mass implicit differentiation (Step 117e) | **Done** | `pseudoMass_deriv_formula`: if `h` satisfies `pseudoMassG α r (h β) = c β` and `HasDerivAt h h' β`, then `h' = c'(β) / g'(h β)`. Proof: chain rule (`HasDerivAt.comp`) + uniqueness of derivatives + `field_simp`. Note: the full Lipschitz estimate (GJ §17.5 p.312) additionally requires the lattice convolution sum bound (Step 117f+). (PR #926 Step 117e.) |
| §17.5 | Pseudo-mass derivative lower bound (Step 117f) | **Done** | `pseudoMassG_deriv_abs_ge (α : ℕ)`: `r · g(t,r,α) ≤ \|g'(t,r,α)\|`. Algebraically: `\|g'\| - r·g = 2·exp(-(tr))·↑α·(tr)^(α-1)·r / (1+(tr)^α)^2 ≥ 0`. Key ingredient for the Lipschitz estimate: combined with Lebowitz bound and lattice convolution sum (Step 117h+), gives `(m⁻)^(2α) · \|dm⁻/dβ\| ≤ const`. (PR #927 Step 117f.) |
| §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 (full) | **Done (qualitative, unconditional on `βJ·2d < 1`, cubic) via the faithful distance-parametrized route** — the full two-sided sandwich `m⁻(σ) ≤ m(σ) ≤ C·m⁻(σ)` now holds unconditionally on the whole high-temperature window against the faithful distance-parametrized `globalPseudoMassDist` (`globalPseudoMassDist_fullSandwich`, #4317): lower side `globalPseudoMassDist_le_latticeMass` (#4314, all high-T / any exhaustion), upper side `latticeMass_le_globalPseudoMassDist_fullUpper` (#4317, `βJ·2d < 1` cubic), and the **exhaustion-independent** (book) form `globalPseudoMassDist_fullSandwich_exhaustion` for an arbitrary exhaustion (#4318, `GlobalPseudoMassDistExhaustion.lean`, via `globalPseudoMassDist_indep_exhaustion` + `latticeMass_indep_cubicExhaustion`). This **supersedes** the old fixed-radius / per-pair-profile route below (whose `hprofile : pseudoMassG α ρ (−log(βJ·2d)) ≤ correlationInfinite {x,z}` ∀-displacement form is provably false — `not_forall_cubicTanhProfileBound_…`, no-go #4270 — because the fixed radius is non-faithful; the genuine inverse correlation length uses the per-pair distance `r = |x−z|`). Only the **sharp** constant `C` (the true transfer-matrix correlation length, item C #4271/#4081) remains out of scope. (issue #4313; #1645 umbrella) | The pseudo-mass / lattice-mass comparison is implemented through the current non-sharp uniform route. Earlier infrastructure built the pseudo-mass calculus, concrete `pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair`, lower-bound transfer, HLS/Lipschitz conditional APIs, derivative-limit provider scaffolding, and path-rate packages. The decisive closure is now the transfer-matrix-style large-`K` route: `lemma_17_5_2_upper_bound_uniform_transfer` gives `∃ K, latticeMass ≤ ofReal K · ofReal m⁻(x,z)` for positive-temperature ferromagnetic active pairs; `lemma_17_5_2_high_temp_sandwich_uniform_transfer` and `_forall` then combine this upper bound with the lower validating-decay input to give the full `ofReal m⁻(x,z) ≤ latticeMass ≤ ofReal K · ofReal m⁻(x,z)` sandwich with one pair-independent constant. The cubic tanh-profile wrapper is conditional only, and `not_forall_cubicTanhProfileBound_of_betaJ_pos_high_temp` shows the all-displacement family is impossible in positive dimension from only `0 < r`, `0 < βJ`, and `βJ·2d < 1`; the direct Simon-Lieb trichotomy bridge is the canonical family-free bridge route. The old derivative-limit / Cauchy route (closed #2931/#2965; future #3026/#3044/#3054) is retained as future refinement infrastructure and is not required for this non-sharp sandwich. References: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311--312. (Part of #1645.) |

| §17.5 | **UNCONDITIONAL correlation profile lower bound at the direct-path rate on the convergence window** (supplies the ratio hypothesis of the rate-agnostic §17.5 Lipschitz engine, not the #4330–#4332 `hprofile` binder uniformly in its radius) | **Done (axiom-free, UNCONDITIONAL; PR #4333)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/UnconditionalProfileLower.lean`. **The per-pair correlation lower bound `pseudoMassG α (dist x z) (−log tanh(βJ)) ≤ ⟨φ_x φ_z⟩^∞` is proven UNCONDITIONALLY** for every distinct pair `x ≠ z` on `β ∈ ConvergenceRegion.window d J` — `pseudoMassG_dist_tanh_rate_le_correlationInfinite_cubic` (general pair) + `..._cubic_zero` (anchored). This is an unconditional correlation lower bound at the **direct-path rate `−log tanh(βJ)`** — the exponent of the GKS direct-path bound. For general `d` the tree relates that rate to the inverse correlation length only from above, by `latticeMass ≤ ofReal(−log tanh(βJ))` (`latticeMass_le_neg_log_tanh_betaJ`, bundled with the Simon–Lieb lower bound in `latticeMass_two_sided_bound`) and by the sharper on-axis `onAxisInverseCorrelationLength ≤ −log tanh(βJ)` (`onAxisInverseCorrelationLength_le_neg_log_tanh`); an equality is proved in `d = 1` only, where `latticeMass_one_eq_correlationMass` gives `latticeMass 1 (cubicExhaustion 1) ⟨J,0,β⟩ = ofReal(correlationMass (βJ))` with `correlationMass a = −log tanh a`. The bound is stated at the pair distance `dist x z` as radius, and at a different rate from the slower Simon–Lieb rate `−log(βJ·2d)` whose ∀-displacement form is false (#4270). Method: (i) `pseudoMassG_le_exp_neg_of_one_le` — `1 ≤ t·r ⇒ pseudoMassG α r t ≤ e^{−tr}`; (ii) on the window `tanh(βJ) < R d ≤ e⁻¹` (`tanh_betaJ_lt_exp_neg_one_of_window`, via `R d = min(…) ≤ 1/(64((2d)²+1)e) ≤ e⁻¹`), so the rate `q := −log tanh(βJ) ≥ 1` (`one_le_neg_log_tanh_betaJ_of_window`), hence `q·dist ≥ 1` and `pseudoMassG α (dist) q ≤ e^{−q·dist} = tanh(βJ)^{dist}`; (iii) the GKS **direct-path** lower bound `twoPointFunction_ge_tanh_betaJ_pow_dist` gives `tanh(βJ)^{dist} ≤ ⟨φ₀φ_z⟩`; (iv) translation invariance (`correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_pair_eq_twoPointFunction`, `latticeDistance_translate_eq`) extends from anchored to general pairs. Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. **Significance:** this does **not** discharge the `hprofile` binder of #4330–#4332 uniformly in that binder's radius. That binder is `pseudoMassG α ρ (−log(βJ·2d)) ≤ correlationInfinite {x,z}` at the **Simon–Lieb rate**, with `ρ` a free radius in #4330/#4331 and the pair distance `latticeDistance d x z` in #4332 — whereas the theorem here is at rate `−log tanh(βJ)` (radius `dist x z`). At #4332's **matched radius** the two statements differ only in the rate and the comparison closes: on the window `tanh(βJ) < βJ ≤ βJ·2d` for `1 ≤ d`, so the direct-path rate is the larger of the two, and `pseudoMassG` is antitone in the rate (`pseudoMassG_antitoneOn`) — the bound proved here is therefore *strictly weaker* than #4332's binder and does not discharge it. For the free-radius #4330/#4331 that rate-only inference is unavailable: `pseudoMassG α r t = 2·e^{−(t·r)}/(1+(t·r)^α)` depends on rate and radius only through the product `t·r`, strictly decreasing in it (`pseudoMassG_strictAntiOn` at `r = 1`), so the theorem here *does* yield their binder at every radius satisfying the criterion `ρ·(−log(βJ·2d)) ≥ dist x z·(−log tanh(βJ))`, and yields it at no radius where that criterion fails. At `ρ = 2·dist x z` the criterion reads `(βJ·2d)² ≤ tanh(βJ)`, which the window supplies with room to spare, and its left-hand side is increasing in `ρ` (the rate `−log(βJ·2d)` is positive on the window, `window_subset_highTemp`), so the criterion is met at every `ρ ≥ 2·dist x z`. What fails there is only the discharge uniform in `ρ`, the form #4330/#4331 are stated in. What it does discharge is the per-pair profile lower bound *at the tanh rate* that supplies the rate-free ratio hypothesis `hratio : Lmin ≤ c(β)/m(β)^{2α}` of the **rate-agnostic** engine `pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair_pow_succ_lipschitz_on_window_of_ratio_lower` (the engine itself carries no rate); re-parametrizing the chain through that engine (#4334) is what yields the **unconditional** finite-region `m⁻(σ,A)^{2α+1}` Lipschitz. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 / Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311–312; GKS direct path. (Issue #4320.) |
| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-1d: per-dart correlation ratio (binding pair) | **Done (axiom-free; PR #4338; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/MassContinuityDartSum.lean`. `correlationInfinite_dart_ratio_le`: for a distinct binding pair `x ≠ y` (`m⁻(x,y) = globalPseudoMassDist`) and an adjacent dart `u ∼ v` (`x≠u`, `y≠v`), `⟨φ_x φ_u⟩·⟨φ_y φ_v⟩/⟨φ_x φ_y⟩ ≤ 2·e^{m⁻}·(1+(m⁻·d(x,y))^α)·(1/(1+(m⁻·d(x,u))^α))·(1/(1+(m⁻·d(y,v))^α))` — the per-term bound of the GJ p.312 sum. Composes #4335 (system-mass majorant + (17.5.3) identity) + #4337 (`pseudoMassG_ratio_le` profile-ratio algebra + `exp_neg_scaled_dart_pair_le_exp` dart exp-cancellation): numerator factors majorized, denominator = profile at the binding pair, ratio-algebra closes. Axiom-free. Next (PR-1e): dart-sum assembly via the fiber decomposition (mirror #4329) + #4336 convolution ⇒ `Σ_dart ⟨xδ⟩⟨yδ⟩/c ≤ const·(1+(m⁻r)^α)(1+r)^{d-2α}`. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |
| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-1e core: c-cancelling incident Lebowitz β-derivative bound | **Done (axiom-free; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/BetaDerivative/LebowitzCancelling.lean`. `summand_le_symmDiff`: the c-cancelling per-summand bound `corr(A△B) − corr(A)·corr(B) ≤ corr(A△B)` (drop the non-negative GKS-I product). `correlation_beta_deriv_le_lebowitz_cancelling`: the c-cancelling variant of `correlation_beta_deriv_le_lebowitz_tight` — the *incident* (degenerate) edges contribute the **reduced** correlation `corr({r,s}△{e})` instead of the coarse `1`, i.e. `∂_β ⟨σ_r σ_s⟩ ≤ J·∑_e leb_e + J·∑_{e incident} corr({r,s}△{e})`. This is GJ's `2A` mechanism: for an incident edge `{r,v}`, `corr({r,s}△{r,v}) = ⟨σ_s σ_v⟩` and `⟨σ_s σ_v⟩/c` stays bounded (`v∼r ⇒ dist(s,v) ≥ dist(r,s)−1`), replacing the loose `J·|incident|/c` that blows up in the p.312 mass-continuity estimate. Method: mirror the tight Lebowitz proof, splitting `edgeFinset` into incident/disjoint; on the disjoint part use `summand_le_lebowitz_of_disjoint`, on the incident part use `summand_le_symmDiff`. Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 proof, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |
| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-1e: cubic c-cancelling finite-volume Lebowitz profile bound | **Done (axiom-free; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/CubicDerivativeProfileCancelling.lean`. `derivative_profile_cubic_le_lebowitz_cancelling`: the c-cancelling counterpart of `derivative_profile_cubic_le_lebowitz_tight` — for `0≤J`, `0<β`, `x≠z`, `{x,z}⊆volume n`, `∂_β c_n ≤ J·∑_{⟨u,v⟩∈E}[⟨σ_xσ_u⟩⟨σ_zσ_v⟩+⟨σ_xσ_v⟩⟨σ_zσ_u⟩] + J·∑_{e incident} corr({⟨x⟩,⟨z⟩}△{e})`, where the incident (degenerate) edges contribute the **reduced** correlation `corr({⟨x⟩,⟨z⟩}△{e})` instead of the coarse `1` (per-edge `card`). This carries GJ's bounded `2A` mechanism (#4339 `correlation_beta_deriv_le_lebowitz_cancelling`) to the cubic finite-volume profile, via `correlationAlongExhaustion_of_subset` + `liftFinset_pair` (same profile-identification as the tight version). Axiom-free. Next: infinite-volume bridge (reduce the incident `corr({⟨x⟩,⟨z⟩}△{e})` to `corr∞{z,v}` / `corr∞{x,v}` and bound `/c` via the single-factor pseudoMassG majorant, `dist(z,v)≥r−1`). Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 proof, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |
| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-1e: c-cancelling infinite-volume incident bridge (non-adjacent pairs) | **Done (axiom-free; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/CubicIncidentInfiniteBridge.lean`. `incident_symmDiff_corr_fin_le_infinite`: per-edge reduction — for **non-adjacent** `x,z` and an incident edge `{u,v}`, `corr_fin({⟨x⟩,⟨z⟩}△{u,v}) ≤ g{x,u}+g{x,v}+g{z,u}+g{z,v}` (`g{a,b} = correlationInfinite … {a,b}`). Non-adjacency ⇒ no edge contains both `⟨x⟩,⟨z⟩`, so exactly one lies in `{u,v}` and the symmDiff collapses to a single 2-point set (via `symmDiff_pair_pair_of_ne`, 4 cases), dominated by `correlation_inducedGraph_cubic_le_correlationInfinite`; other terms nonneg. `derivative_profile_cubic_le_infiniteVolume_lebowitz_cancelling`: c-cancelling counterpart of `derivative_profile_cubic_le_infiniteVolume_lebowitz` — `∂_β c_n ≤ J·∑_{⟨u,v⟩∈E}[g{x,u}g{z,v}+g{x,v}g{z,u}] + J·∑_{e incident}(g{x,u}+g{x,v}+g{z,u}+g{z,v})`; the incident `J·|count|` of the tight version (blows up `/c`) is replaced by the infinite-volume reduced cross sum (GJ's bounded `2A`). Composes #4340 + the per-edge bridge + the Lebowitz finite≤infinite bridge. Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Next: single-factor `/c` bound (`g{z,v}/c ≤ const` for `v∼x`, `dist(z,v)≥r−1`, #4335 majorant) + dart-sum `/c` assembly (#4336+#4338) ⇒ `hcomp`. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 proof, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |

| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-1i: unified per-dart `/c` bound | **Done (axiom-free; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/MassContinuityDartDivC.lean`. `dart_term_div_c_le`: for a distinct binding pair `x≠z` and **any** dart `dt` of the induced cubic graph, the ordered product `⟨φ_xφ_{dt.fst}⟩·⟨φ_zφ_{dt.snd}⟩/⟨φ_xφ_z⟩ ≤ 2(1+(m⁻d(x,z))^α)·(1/(1+(m⁻d(x,dt.fst))^α))·(1/(1+(m⁻d(z,dt.snd))^α))·e^{m⁻}`. Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Next: sum over darts (finite Dart-sum → infinite tsum, `Finset.sum_le_tsum`) + #4336 m⁻-scaled convolution ⇒ `cross-sum/c ≤ const·(1+(m⁻r)^α)(1+r)^{d−2α}`, then `hcomp`. Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Next: dart-profile sum → infinite-lattice convolution (`Finset.sum_le_tsum` + #4336) ⇒ `≤ const·(1+(m⁻r)^α)(1+r)^{d−2α}`, then `hcomp`. | §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-1i: dart-sum-by-neighbor grouping (general infrastructure) | **Done (axiom-free; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/DartSumNeighborGrouping.lean`. `SimpleGraph.sum_dart_eq_sum_neighborFinset`: for a finite simple graph `G` and `F : V→V→ℝ`, `∑_{d:Dart} F(d.fst,d.snd) = ∑_v ∑_{w∈neighborFinset v} F(v,w)`. General fiber-decomposition (dart analog of `Finset.sum_fiberwise`): groups the dart sum by `d.fst` (`Finset.sum_fiberwise_of_maps_to`), identifies each fibre with the neighbour set (`dart_fst_fiber` + `dartOfNeighborSet` injective), converts the subtype sum to a `neighborFinset` sum (`Finset.sum_subtype`). Converts the PR-1i cross-sum's dart-profile sum (#4347) into the neighbour-shifted double sum consumed by the m⁻-scaled HLS convolution (#4336). Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Next: box→lattice neighbor embedding + `Finset.sum_le_tsum` + #4336 ⇒ `cross-sum/c ≤ const·(1+(m⁻r)^α)(1+r)^{d−2α}`. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 proof, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |

Applies the dart-sum grouping #4348 (group by `dt.fst`), factors `s(x,v.val)` out (`Finset.mul_sum`), and bounds each induced-box neighbour sum by the **lattice** neighbour sum (box neighbours inject via `.val` into lattice neighbours: `Finset.sum_image` + `Finset.sum_le_sum_of_subset_of_nonneg`, using `inducedGraph.Adj v w → latticeGraph.Adj v.val w.val`). Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Next: box-vertex sum → infinite-lattice tsum (`Finset.sum_le_tsum`) + #4336 ⇒ `≤ const·(1+(m⁻r)^α)(1+r)^{d−2α}`. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 proof, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |

(Issue #4320.) |

| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-1k: bounded pseudo-mass denominator ratio (GJ-faithful `2A`) | **Done (axiom-free; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/MassContinuityDenomRatio.lean`. `pseudoMass_denom_ratio_le`: for `0≤m`, `2≤r`, `r−1≤s`, `(1+(m·r)^α)/(1+(m·s)^α) ≤ 1+2^α`. The arithmetic enabler for the **GJ-faithful bounded** incident "`2A`" term (correcting the loose `(1+(m⁻r)^α)` of #4343/#4344): for a non-adjacent binding pair (`r=d(x,z)≥2`), an incident dart `v∼x` has `s=d(z,v)≥r−1≥r/2`, so `r≤2s` and `(m⁻r)^α≤2^α(m⁻s)^α`, giving the bounded ratio. Per GJ p.312, the incident term must keep the per-incident-dart denominator (#4342) and use this bound — yielding a `dist`-independent constant `2A`, not the unbounded `(1+(m⁻r)^α)`. Proof: `r≤2s` + `pow_le_pow_left₀` + `nlinarith`. Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Next (PR-1l): tight incident `/c` bound `≤ 4d·(1+2^α)·e^{m⁻}` (constant) via #4342 + this; then re-combine with the cross bound (which is already correct) ⇒ `|c'|≤const·dist·c/(m⁻)^{2α}` ⇒ uniform Lipschitz ⇒ system continuity ⇒ capstone. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 proof, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |
| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-1l: GJ-faithful bounded per-edge incident `/c` bound | **Done (axiom-free; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/MassContinuityIncidentDivCTight.lean`. `incident_symmDiff_corr_fin_div_c_le_tight`: for a non-adjacent binding pair `x≠z` and an incident edge `{u,v}`, `corr_fin({⟨x⟩,⟨z⟩}△{u,v})/⟨φ_xφ_z⟩ ≤ (1+2^α)·e^{m⁻}` — a **constant** (independent of the edge and of `d(x,z)`), the GJ p.312 bounded `2A`. Corrects #4343 (which dropped the per-incident-dart denominator, leaving the unbounded `(1+(m⁻r)^α)`): here the denominator is kept and bounded via #4353 `pseudoMass_denom_ratio_le` using `r=d(x,z)≥2` (non-adjacency) and `d(z,v)≥r−1` (`v∼x`, triangle). | §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — bridge: `globalPseudoMassDist = inf over the cubic exhaustion` | **Done (axiom-free; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/GlobalPseudoMassDistCubicInf.lean`. `globalPseudoMassDist_eq_csInf_finiteRegion_cubic`: for `0<J`, `0<β`, `globalPseudoMassDist hα (cubicExhaustion d) ⟨J,0,β⟩ = sInf (range (n ↦ finiteRegionPseudoMassDist hα (cubicExhaustion d) ⟨J,0,β⟩ (volume n) n.2))` over `cubicMassIndex d` (the stages with a distinct pair). The Lean form of GJ p.312's `m⁻(σ)=inf_A m⁻(σ,A)=lim_{A↑} m⁻(σ,A)`: the system pseudo-mass (sInf over all active pairs) equals the inf over cubic stages of the finite-region pseudo-masses (each a finite attained `inf'`). `≤`: finite-region inf' attained at an in-box (active) pair ⇒ `globalPseudoMassDist_le_of_active`; `le_csInf`. `≥`: any active pair `(x,z)∈volume N` for large N (`Exhaustion.exhaust`) ⇒ `finiteRegion(volume N) ≤ pseudoMass(x,z)` (`Finset.inf'_le`) ⇒ lower bound of `globalPseudoMassDistSet`; `le_csInf`. Helpers: `cubicMassIndex`, `finiteRegionDistinctPairs_cubicVolume_one_nonempty` (witness `(0,Pi.single ⟨0,_⟩ 1)` for d≥1). This is the bridge that lets the Step-2 inf-Lipschitz lemma act on the genuine `globalPseudoMassDist`. Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Next: power-commutes-with-sInf + Step-1 (uniform-in-A finite-region sharp Lipschitz) ⇒ Thm 17.5.1. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 proof, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |
| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — Step-2: an infimum of uniformly-Lipschitz functions is Lipschitz | **Done (axiom-free; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/MassContinuityUniformInfLipschitz.lean`. `abs_csInf_range_sub_csInf_range_le` (+ `abs_csInf_envelope_sub_le_of_uniform_lipschitz`): for families `fa,fb:ι→ℝ` with bounded-below ranges and `|fb i−fa i|≤L` for all `i`, `|sInf(range fb)−sInf(range fa)|≤L`. Pure `sInf` algebra (`sInf(range fa)−L` is a lower bound of `range fb` ⇒ `le_csInf`; symmetric). This is the mathematical core of GJ's `A↑ℝ^d` step (p.312): GJ proves `m⁻(σ,A)^{2α+1}` Lipschitz with constant **uniform in the bounded region A**, then `m⁻(σ)=inf_A m⁻(σ,A)`; passing the uniform Lipschitz bound through the inf over A needs no differentiation and **no attainment of the infimum**. This **dissolves the apparent "binding-pair existence" obstruction** (#4320): one takes the inf over the *finite, attained* regions A of functions that are already (uniformly) Lipschitz, not the un-attained inf over pairs. Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Next: bridge `globalPseudoMassDist^{2α+1}=sInf(range finiteRegion^{2α+1})` + Step-1 (uniform-in-A finite-region sharp Lipschitz) ⇒ Thm 17.5.1. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 proof, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |
| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-A: Lipschitz of a lower envelope via pointwise binding derivatives | **Done (axiom-free; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/MassContinuityInfEnvelopeLipschitz.lean`. `abs_sub_le_of_isInf_binding_deriv` (+ one-sided helper `le_add_mul_sub_of_isInf_binding_deriv`): pure real-analysis fencing lemma — if `g` is continuous on `Icc a b`, lies below each `f i`, and at every `x` *equals* some `f i` differentiable there with `|deriv|≤M`, then `|g b−g a|≤M·(b−a)`. This is the GJ p.312 mechanism for differentiating the system pseudo-mass `m⁻(σ)=inf over pairs` directly (an infimum is pinned to a binding pair but is not itself differentiable). Engine: Mathlib `image_le_of_liminf_slope_right_le_deriv_boundary` with affine boundary `B x=g a+M(x−a)` (binding equality + domination ⇒ right-slope of `g` ≤ right-slope of `f i` → `deriv(f i)x≤M`); backward direction by the reflection `t↦a+b−t`. Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 proof, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |

| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-C: global pseudo-mass-power Lipschitz (conditional capstone) | **Partial (axiom-free; conditional on binding-existence + envelope continuity; toward Thm 17.5.1) — superseded by PR #4406** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/MassContinuityGlobalEnvelopeLipschitz.lean`. `globalPseudoMassDist_pow_succ_lipschitz_on_Icc_of_binding_deriv`: on `Icc β₁ β₂` (`0<β₁`), **given** (1) `hcont` — continuity of the envelope `β↦(m⁻(β))^{2α+1}`, and (2) `hbind_deriv` — at every `β` a globally-binding active pair `x≠z` with `|deriv((m⁻(x,z,·))^{2α+1})|≤M` (uniform `M`), the system pseudo-mass power is `M`-Lipschitz: `|(m⁻(β₂))^{2α+1}−(m⁻(β₁))^{2α+1}|≤M·(β₂−β₁)`. Direct application of PR-A: family `f q β=(m⁻(q.1,q.2,β))^{2α+1}` over **distinct** pairs (subtype; diagonal pairs give `0`, breaking domination); domination `g≤f q` from `globalPseudoMassDist_le_of_active` (every distinct pair active at `β>0`) + `pow_le_pow_left₀`; binding data from `hbind_deriv` (PR-B1 supplies the derivative given a binding pair). **Conditional/Partial** — two genuine gaps (#4320, "transfer-principle-blocked"): G1 = binding-pair existence (`sInf`-over-pairs *attainment*; in the Ornstein–Zernike regime `m_{x,z}↓m∞` as `d(x,z)→∞`, so the inf is approached but not attained by a finite pair) and G2 = envelope continuity (`m⁻` a priori only USC); plus the uniform bound `M` (the p.312 `(2α+1)·S·(m⁻)^{2α}/d(x,z)≤M`, bounded for `α≥d−1`, with β-uniform `C` still to be assembled). **NOTE: This row is now superseded by the unconditional §17.5.1 rigorous-content capstone (PR #4406, `gj_theorem_17_5_1_rigorous`, ~row 1893, `MassContinuityLatticeMassWindow.lean`), which proves `m⁻` continuity, Lemma 17.5.2 sandwich, and true-mass continuity off a countable set, all unconditional on binding-pair existence and envelope continuity.** Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 proof, pp. 311–312. (Issue #4320.) |
| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-1c: dart exp-cancellation + pseudoMassG profile-ratio algebra | **Done (axiom-free; PR #4337; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/MassContinuityDartRatio.lean`. Two per-dart building blocks of the GJ p.312 ratio: `exp_neg_scaled_dart_pair_le_exp` (edge analog of #4329: for adjacent `u∼v`, `exp(−t(d(x,u)+d(y,v)−d(x,y))) ≤ exp t` via the triangle through the edge, `d(x,y) ≤ d(x,u)+1+d(y,v)`), and `pseudoMassG_ratio_le` (profile-ratio algebra: `P(a)P(b)/P(c) = 2(1+(mc)^α)·s_a·s_b·e^{−m(a+b−c)} ≤ 2(1+(mc)^α)·s_a·s_b·e^m` for `m,a,b,c ≥ 0`, `s_r = 1/(1+(mr)^α)`, given the exp bound). Combined with #4335 (majorant/identity) + #4336 (dart scaled convolution) these give the per-dart correlation ratio and the dart-sum/c bound (PR-1d). Axiom-free. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |
| §17.5 | GJ Theorem 17.5.1 proof — PR-1b: m⁻-scaled neighbour-shift (dart) HLS convolution | **Done (axiom-free; PR #4336; toward Thm 17.5.1)** | `IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/MassContinuityDartScaledHLS.lean`. `tsum_mul_neighborFinset_sum_scaled_le`: for `t>0`, `d/2<α<d`, `∃C>0, ∀x z, ∑'_u 1/(1+(t·d(x,u))^α)·(∑_{v∼u}1/(1+(t·d(z,v))^α)) ≤ C·(1+d(x,z))^{−(2α−d)}` — the `t=m⁻` scaled, edge/dart form of the HLS convolution (the Ising β-derivative Lebowitz bound is a nearest-neighbour edge cross-sum, not GJ's site sum). Each scaled kernel reduces to `Ct·(1+d)^{−α}` (`one_div_one_add_M_t_pow_le_const_mul_one_div_one_add_pow_pow`), so the bound follows from the unscaled neighbour-shift convolution `tsum_mul_neighborFinset_sum_pow_neg_le` (#4327); `C = Ct²·C₄₃₂₇`. Axiom-free. This is the convolution at the heart of the GJ p.312 derivative-ratio bound; combined with the #4335 majorant + identity + triangle exp-cancellation it gives the dart-sum/c ratio. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1, p. 312. (Issue #4320.) |

These feed the p.312 derivative-ratio bound (sharp HLS #4325–#4329 + triangle exp-cancellation), whose linear-in-dist scaling gives the A-uniform Lipschitz constant (K = const·dist), hence m⁻(σ) continuity and (Lemma 17.5.2 sandwich #4317) m(σ) continuity. Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Roadmap (PR-1 ratio bound → PR-2 per-pair uniform Lipschitz → PR-3 uniform finite-region → PR-4 global continuity → PR-5 capstone) in #4320. Reference: GJ §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1, pp. 311–312. (Issue #4320.) |

Axiom-free `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. **Conditional/Partial:** per-pair `hprofile` faithful distance form (∀-displacement false, #4270); the constant is **per-`A`** — uniform-in-`A` / infinite-envelope `globalPseudoMassDist` continuity does **not** follow (per-pair Lipschitz constant (2α+1)K/dist uncontrolled as diam A→∞, #4320). Helper lemmas: `spinProduct_mul_totalMagnetization` (σ^A·M = Σ_i σ^{AΔ{i}}), `gibbsExpectation_spinProd_mul_mag` (⟨σ^A·M⟩ = Σ_i ⟨σ^{AΔ{i}}⟩), `gibbsExpectation_totalMag_eq_sum` (⟨M⟩ = Σ_i⟨σ_i⟩). Proof: GKS-II gives `⟨σ^{AΔ{i}}⟩ ≥ ⟨σ^A⟩·⟨σ_i⟩` term-by-term. Infinitesimal form underlying monotonicity of correlations in h. Reference: GJ §4.2 Prop. 4.2.1 (p. 58), applied to the singleton couplings; GJ §17.6 pp. 348–351. (PR #935 Step 121.) |

| §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 Part B upper bound closed via large-`K` uniform transfer | **Done (unconditional positive-temperature upper bound `m ≤ const·m⁻`, non-sharp constant)** | `exists_pos_forall_pseudoMassG_ge_one_near_zero`, `exists_K_forall_pseudoMassG_ge_one_of_rate_bound`, `exists_uniform_transfer_pseudoMassG_of_rate_bound`, `neg_log_tanh_betaJ_nonneg`, `admissible_rate_le_neg_log_tanh`, `exists_uniform_transfer_pseudoMassG`, and `lemma_17_5_2_upper_bound_uniform_transfer` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/UniformTransferLargeK.lean`) discharge the uniform `pseudoMassG` transfer bound `Lemma_17_5_2_UniformTransferPseudoMassGBound`, closing the upper-bound side of GJ Lemma 17.5.2 `m(σ) ≤ const·m⁻(σ)` for ferromagnetic positive-temperature active pairs (`0 < d, J, β`, `1 ≤ α`, `0 < r`). Mechanism: `pseudoMassG α r` is continuous with value `2` at `0` and antitone, so it stays `≥ 1` on a neighbourhood `[0, δ]`; every infinite-volume correlation is uniformly `≤ 1` (`correlationInfinite_le_one`, the uniform gap below `2`) and every admissible decay rate is `≤ -log(tanh(βJ))` (`HasExponentialDecay_rate_le_neg_log_tanh_betaJ`, transferred to an arbitrary exhaustion), so a single `K` (depending only on `α, r, d, J, β`, not on the rate or pair) forces `(a:ℝ)/K ≤ δ`, giving `correlationInfinite ≤ 1 ≤ pseudoMassG α r ((a:ℝ)/K)`. `lemma_17_5_2_upper_bound_uniform_transfer` then yields `∃ K, latticeMass ≤ ofReal K · ofReal m⁻(x,z)`. The constant is uniform but **not sharp**: this proves the qualitative GJ statement (existence of a constant) via the uniform pseudo-mass lower bound `m⁻(x,z) ≥ pseudoMassG⁻¹(1)` that `correlationInfinite ≤ 1` yields, not the sharp transfer-matrix correlation length. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311--312. (Part of #3378, Part of #1645.) |

Distinct from the pair-dependent-constant `lemma_17_5_2_cubic_high_temp_sandwich_capstone`; the constant is uniform but **not sharp** (not the transfer-matrix correlation length). **NOTE**: this row is now superseded by the unconditional PR #4406 rows (~1893/~1904), which prove the full sandwich without the profile hypothesis. | §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 lower bound `m⁻(σ) ≤ m(σ)` — **faithful, UNCONDITIONAL** | **Done (unconditional)** | `globalPseudoMassDist_le_latticeMass` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/GlobalPseudoMassDist.lean`): `ENNReal.ofReal (globalPseudoMassDist hα Λ ⟨J,0,β⟩) ≤ latticeMass d Λ ⟨J,0,β⟩` for `1 ≤ α`, `0 ≤ J`, `0 < β` — **no profile hypothesis**. This is the genuine GJ §17.5 Lemma 17.5.2 lower bound (book pp. 311–312): the pseudo-mass `m⁻(σ) = inf` over active distinct pairs of the **distance-parametrized** per-pair pseudo-mass `pseudoMassFromParamsAtPairDist` (radius `r = latticeDistance d x z`, the inverse-correlation-length scale the book uses) is bounded by the true mass `m(σ) = latticeMass`. Proof = the book's existential near-optimal-pair argument: for any rate `R` above `latticeMass.toReal`, `¬ HasExponentialDecay R` yields (at `C = 2`) a single active pair with `correlationInfinite {i,j} > 2·exp(−R·dist)` (`truncated2Infinite_h_zero` + `correlationInfinite_nonneg`); aligning the profile radius with that pair's distance gives `pseudoMassG α dist R ≤ 2·exp(−R·dist) < corr` (`pseudoMassG_le_two_mul_exp`), so `pseudoMass_le_iff_pseudoMassG_le` ⇒ the per-pair distance pseudo-mass `≤ R`, hence `globalPseudoMassDist = sInf ≤ R`; squeeze `R ↓ latticeMass.toReal`. The witness is ONE large-correlation pair (the book's existential choice), NOT a ∀-pair bound, which is why it sidesteps the no-go #4270 that kills the fixed-`r` profile route. `#print axioms` = `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. **This discharges the lower-bound gap** that the fixed-`r` `globalPseudoMass`/`hprofile` route (rows above) could not (that route is non-faithful: fixed `r` makes `globalPseudoMass ≤ latticeMass` FALSE — `m⁻ → ∞` as `r → 0`). Re-basing the full sandwich (incl. the upper side and `Lemma_17_5_2_GlobalAllRateComparison`) on `globalPseudoMassDist` is a tracked follow-up (#4313). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311–312. (Issue #4313, Part of #4214 item C / #1645.) |
| §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 **faithful sandwich** `m⁻(σ) ≤ m(σ) ≤ C·m⁻(σ)` on the strict-high-temp cubic window — **Done (unconditional on `βJ·2d < 1/2`, cubic)** | `globalPseudoMassDist_restrictedSandwich` (`Lemma_17_5_2/GlobalPseudoMassDistUpper.lean`): for `1 ≤ α`, `0 < d`, `0 < J`, `0 < β`, `βJ·2d < 1/2`, `ENNReal.ofReal (globalPseudoMassDist …) ≤ latticeMass d (cubicExhaustion d) ⟨J,0,β⟩ ≤ ENNReal.ofReal C · ENNReal.ofReal (globalPseudoMassDist …)` with `C = (−log(tanh(βJ)))/M` pair-uniform — **both sides against the faithful distance-parametrized `globalPseudoMassDist`**. | §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 **faithful FULL-window sandwich (cubic)** `m⁻(σ) ≤ m(σ) ≤ C·m⁻(σ)` on the WHOLE high-temp window `βJ·2d < 1` — **Done (unconditional on `βJ·2d < 1`, cubic)** | `globalPseudoMassDist_fullSandwich`, `latticeMass_le_globalPseudoMassDist_fullUpper`, `pseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge_of_high_temp_full`, `globalPseudoMassDistFullRate`, `pseudoMassG_one_le_of_le_third`, `pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair_zero_le_of_corr_le_one_adjacent` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/GlobalPseudoMassDistUpperFull.lean`). **Extends the `βJ·2d < 1/2` restricted sandwich (#4315) to the FULL high-temperature window `βJ·2d < 1`**, both sides against the faithful distance-parametrized `globalPseudoMassDist`. The `1/2` barrier was an artefact: the restricted route discharged the Simon–Lieb trichotomy's *adjacent* (`dist=1`) branch through the finite-susceptibility ceiling `corr ≤ B = βJ·2d/(1−βJ·2d)` + rate `M ≤ −log B`, forcing `B < 1` (`βJ·2d < 1/2`). But the bridge's `bound` field only needs `corr ≤ pseudoMassG α r (M·dist/r)`, which at `dist=1` is `corr ≤ 2·exp(−M)/(1+M^α)`; since `corr_adjacent ≤ 1` ALWAYS (`correlationInfinite_le_one`) and `2·exp(−M)/(1+M^α) ≥ 1` for `M ≤ 1/3` (`(1+M^α)·exp(M) ≤ (1+M)/(1−M) ≤ 2` via `Real.add_one_le_exp`; `pseudoMassG_one_le_of_le_third`), the adjacent branch is discharged UNCONDITIONALLY. The `dist ≥ 2` branches already use Simon–Lieb decay on the whole `βJ·2d < 1`. So the pair-uniform rate `M = min (1/3) (simonLiebRate β J d / (2(α+1)))` (`globalPseudoMassDistFullRate`) discharges all three trichotomy branches on `βJ·2d < 1`, giving `globalPseudoMassDist ≥ M` and hence `latticeMass ≤ (−log(tanh(βJ))/M)·globalPseudoMassDist`. This is the **qualitative** Lemma 17.5.2 upper bound (the existence of a finite `C`); the SHARP `C` (the true transfer-matrix correlation length, item C #4271) remains out of scope. `#print axioms` = `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311–312. (Issue #4313, Part of #4214 item C / #1645.) |
| §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 (a)+(b) **finite-region pseudo-mass `m⁻(σ,A)` continuity in β + strict positivity, ANY exhaustion** — **Done (unconditional on the high-temp window, arbitrary exhaustion)** | `finiteRegionPseudoMassDist`, `finiteRegionPseudoMassDist_beta_continuousAt_of_high_temp`, `finiteRegionPseudoMassDist_beta_continuousOn_high_temp`, `finiteRegionPseudoMassDist_pos_of_betaJ_pos`, `correlationInfinite_pair_active_of_betaJ_pos_exhaustion` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/FiniteRegionPseudoMassDistContinuity.lean`). Formalizes parts (a) (continuity) and (b) (strict positivity) of GJ §17.5 Lemma 17.5.2 (pp. 311–312) for an **arbitrary exhaustion `Λ`** (the book's exhaustion-independent setting): for a **bounded** region `A` (with at least one distinct pair, `(finiteRegionDistinctPairs A).Nonempty`) the system pseudo-mass `m⁻(σ,A)` is continuous in the inverse temperature, and strictly positive. The book's continuous object is the *finite* infimum over the distinct pairs of a fixed bounded `A` — `finiteRegionPseudoMassDist hα Λ p A hA := (finiteRegionDistinctPairs A).inf' hA (fun q => pseudoMassFromParamsAtPairDist hα Λ p q.1 q.2)` — using the faithful distance-parametrized per-pair pseudo-mass of #4314 (radius `r = latticeDistance d x z`). A finite `Finset.inf'` of continuous functions is continuous (`ContinuousAt.finset_inf'_apply`), unlike the infinite lower envelope `globalPseudoMassDist` whose continuity is not automatic. Single-pair continuity: `pseudoMassExt_continuousAt` ∘ `correlationInfinite_continuousAt_beta_of_high_temp` (already valid for any `Λ`), with active-range membership `Ioo 0 2` from `correlationInfinite_pair_active_of_betaJ_pos_exhaustion` (cubic active lemma transported by `correlationInfinite_indep_exhaustion`), valid on `β ∈ Ioo 0 (1/(J·2d))`. Strict positivity: `Finset.lt_inf'_iff` + `pseudoMass_pos`. `#print axioms` = `[propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. (Generalized from the original cubic statement #4316 to any exhaustion #4319.) Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311–312. (Issue #4313, Part of #4214 item C / #1645.) |
| §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 uniform sandwich from cubic tanh-profile inputs | **Done (profile input supplied by displacement tanh profile)** | `pseudoMassG_le_correlationInfinite_of_cubicTanhProfileBound_displacement`, `activePseudoMassPair_of_cubicTanhProfileBound_displacement`, `lemma_17_5_2_high_temp_sandwich_uniform_transfer_of_cubicTanhProfileBound`, and `lemma_17_5_2_high_temp_sandwich_uniform_transfer_forall_of_cubicTanhProfileBound` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/UniformSandwichCubicTanh.lean`) compose the uniform-sandwich theorem with the existing cubic tanh-profile infrastructure. The named input `cubicTanhProfileBound α d r β J (z - x)` supplies the anchored lower bound `pseudoMassG α r (-log(βJ·2d)) ≤ tanh(βJ)^dist(0,z-x)`; the path lower bound gives the cubic anchored correlation bound, translation invariance changes `{0,z-x}` to `{x,z}`, and exhaustion-independence transfers the cubic correlation to an arbitrary target exhaustion. The same input supplies active-range membership, so the final theorem removes the raw profile hypothesis from the PR #3383 uniform sandwich. The `_forall` form uses one `K` for every distinct pair when the tanh-profile bound holds for every nonzero displacement, but that all-displacement family is only a conditional interface: the no-go facts below show it cannot be discharged in positive dimension from `0 < r`, `0 < βJ`, and `βJ·2d < 1`. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311--312; Simon 1980, Comm. Math. Phys. 77, 111--126; Lieb 1980, Comm. Math. Phys. 77, 127--135. (Part of #1645.) |
| §17.5 | Cubic tanh-profile all-displacement no-go | **Done (formal obstruction to a beta-only profile-family discharge)** | `latticeDistance_firstAxis_eq`, `firstAxis_succ_ne_zero`, `not_forall_cubicTanhProfileBound_of_tanh_pos_lt_one`, and `not_forall_cubicTanhProfileBound_of_betaJ_pos_high_temp` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/CubicPseudoMassTanhProfileNoGo.lean`) record that the all-displacement family `∀ w ≠ 0, cubicTanhProfileBound α d r β J w` cannot hold in positive dimension when `0 < tanh(βJ) < 1` and the rate `-log(βJ·2d)` is nonnegative; in particular it is impossible under `0 < r`, `0 < βJ`, and `βJ·2d < 1`. Along a coordinate axis, the right-hand side `tanh(βJ)^dist(0,w)` tends to zero, while `pseudoMassG α r (-log(βJ·2d))` remains strictly positive. This clarifies that the `_forall_of_cubicTanhProfileBound` sandwich wrapper is a conditional API rather than a path to an input-free high-temperature sandwich in positive dimension from only `0 < r`, `0 < βJ`, and `βJ·2d < 1`. (Part of #1645.) |
| §17.5 / §5.1 | Lemma 17.5.2 unconditional adjacent-pair susceptibility bridge (audit B1, #4268) | **Done (unconditional `PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge` + HLS pair-product sum in `0 < βJ·2d < 1/2`)** | `correlationInfinite_le_susceptibility_bound_of_high_temp` and `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_susceptibility_bound_of_high_temp` (`Inequalities/HighTemp/SummabilityCluster.lean`): from the finite high-temperature susceptibility ceiling (B7 #4275), every nonnegative pair term obeys `correlationInfinite {i,w} ≤ βJ·2d/(1−βJ·2d) =: B` (single term ≤ whole susceptibility sum, GKS-II, via the private singleton `sum_correlationAlongExhaustion_le_susceptibilityAlongExhaustion` + `susceptibilityAlongExhaustion_le_of_high_temp` + `le_of_tendsto`). `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_pair_le_exp_neg_of_high_temp`, `pseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge_of_high_temp`, `tsum_correlationInfinite_pair_product_le_const_of_high_temp`, and `tsum_correlationInfinite_pair_product_diagonal_le_const_of_high_temp` (`Lemma_17_5_2/HLSBridgeUnconditional.lean`): in the strict window `0 < βJ·2d < 1/2` we have `0 < B < 1`, so `M := min(min 1 (−log B)) (simonLiebRate β J d /(2(α+1)))` is positive and simultaneously satisfies `M ≤ 1`, `(α+1)M ≤ simonLiebRate/2`, and the adjacent bound `correlationInfinite {0,w} ≤ exp(−M)` — the distance-1 case the Simon–Lieb peeling could only bound by `1`. Feeding this into `PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge_of_simonLieb_trichotomy_adjacent` produces an **unconditional** `PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge`, hence the §17.5 HLS pair-product sum bound `∃ K>0, ∑'_z corr{x₀,z}·corr{y₀,z} ≤ K` with no adjacent-decay hypothesis. This discharges the audit B1 adjacent-bridge gap (#4268) by an unconditional theorem. **Scope caveat:** the discharge holds on the strict sub-window `0 < βJ·2d < 1/2` (where the susceptibility ceiling `B < 1`); the wider Simon–Lieb band `1/2 ≤ βJ·2d < 1` is intrinsically not reached by this method (there `B ≥ 1`, so the ceiling gives no `exp(−M) < 1` adjacent bound) and still rests on an assumed adjacent input. The §17.5 *sharp* HLS constant (#4271 / item C) remains separate. Axiom-free. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 pp. 311--312, §5.1 pp. 73--74; Friedli--Velenik §3.7.3. (Closes #4268, Part of #4267 / #4214.) |
| §17.5 | Theorem 17.5.1 / Lemma 17.5.2 SHARP HLS two-sided sandwich (audit B4 #4271 / B2 #4269, master #4214 item C) | **Done (axiom-free, modulo the per-pair profile hypothesis)** | `lemma_17_5_2_sandwich_sharp_cubicExhaustion` (`Lemma_17_5_2/SharpHLSCapstone.lean`) gives the GJ §17.5 sharp two-sided sandwich `m⁻(x,z) ≤ m(x,z) ≤ (2α+1)·K/ρ · m⁻(x,z)` (one HLS constant `K>0`) for the cubic-exhaustion infinite-volume mass at high temperature (`1≤α`, `2α>d`, `1≤d`, `0<J`, `x≠z`, `Icc β₁ β₂ ⊆ ConvergenceRegion.window d J`, `0<β₂`, `β₂·J·2d<1`). The derivative-limit input — locally-uniform convergence of the finite-stage β-derivatives (the Montel / Vitali–Porter normal-families core) — is now supplied **axiom-free** by `ConvergenceRegion.derivativeLimit_on_window` (#4296/#4297, which discharged the former scope-excluded axiom `lemma_17_5_2_derivativeLimitProvider_latticeGraph` and deleted the module that declared it, `Lemma_17_5_2.SharpHLSScopeExcludedAxioms`, in commit `a8556e58`; that module is not archived — the axiom it declared is discharged), at the cost of rescoping the capstone from the formal interval `Ioo 0 (1/(J·2d))` to the genuine convergence window `ConvergenceRegion.window d J ⊆ Ioo 0 (1/(J·2d))`. The remaining non-elementary input is the **per-pair profile lower-bound hypothesis** `pseudoMassG α ρ (−log(β₂·J·2d)) ≤ correlationInfinite {x,z}` — the same validating-decay input the non-sharp uniform sandwich carries, kept as a hypothesis (and the decay *proven* from it via `HasExponentialDecay_pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair_of_pseudoMassG_le_corr`) rather than axiomatized, because its unconditional `∀ x≠z` form is provably **false** (no-go B3 #4270: far pairs would force `latticeMass = ⊤`). `#print axioms lemma_17_5_2_sandwich_sharp_cubicExhaustion = [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. This discharges audit B4 #4271 (sharp constant) and B2 #4269 (the CE route-input it required). Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 / Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311--312. (Closes #4271, #4269; Part of #4267 / #4214 item C.) |
| §17.5 | Theorem 17.5.1 Brick 1: linear log-Lipschitz per-pair estimate (binding pairs, `d≥3`, issue #4386) | **Done (axiom-free, binding-pair scope only)** | `abs_deriv_log_correlationInfinite_le_dist_of_binding` (`Lemma_17_5_2/MassContinuityLogDerivBindingLinear.lean`): a progress brick toward the full hLogLip hypothesis for Theorem 17.5.1. For `d≥3`, non-adjacent pairs `x≠z`, binding-pair hypothesis `hbind` (pair attains global pseudo-mass infimum, α=d−1), at `β∈ConvergenceRegion.window d J`, the β-log-derivative of the infinite-volume two-point function is bounded linearly in separation: `∃K≥0, |∂_β log⟨φ_x φ_z⟩^∞| ≤ K·d(x,z)`. The key linearization step: exponent `α=d−1` (the unique choice making the sharp ratio bound `S` scale as `r`) ensures the growth `(m^−·r)^{d−1}` against decay `(1+r)^{−(d−2)}` is absorbed into a linear term via `sharp_growth_decay_le_linear`. From `abs_deriv_correlationInfinite_le_sharp` (the sharp ratio bound, non-binding-gated), passing to the log-derivative via `HasDerivAt.log` (using positivity `⟨⟩>0`), then the linearization gives the per-pair bound; integration to uniform constants is Brick 2 (deferred). `#print axioms abs_deriv_log_correlationInfinite_le_dist_of_binding = [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]`. **Scope caveat:** this discharges hLogLip **only for binding pairs** (`d≥3`); the general non-binding case (the OZ sharp-lower-bound gap) is multi-session work (#4386, §18.6–18.7 walk-sum content), deferred. Theorem 17.5.1 continuity remains incomplete pending the non-binding generalization. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1, p.~312. (Part of #4386.) |

| §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 high-temperature ratio-lower package | **Done (conditional ratio-lower upper/sandwich assembly)** | `lemma_17_5_2_finite_deriv_bound_of_high_temp_scalar_bound`, `lemma_17_5_2_upper_bound_of_high_temp_ratio_lower`, and `lemma_17_5_2_sandwich_of_high_temp_ratio_lower` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/FiniteHLSLipschitzPackage/RatioLower.lean`): factors the fixed-`K` high-temperature derivative handoff out of the provider theorem, then chooses one enlarged constant `K = max K₀ (max (path*r/((2α+1)*m⁻)) (B/L))`, where `B = J*M^2 + J*4d` and `L` is an eventual lower bound for `correlationAlongExhaustion / h^(2α)` on the interval. This same `K` carries the HLS convolution estimate, discharges the Step 115 path-rate comparison, and supplies the finite derivative bound from the high-temperature Lebowitz/susceptibility estimate. | §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 uniform-limit ratio-lower package | **Done (conditional uniform-correlation-to-sandwich assembly)** | `lemma_17_5_2_ratio_lower_of_uniform_correlation_limit`, `lemma_17_5_2_ratio_lower_of_uniform_correlation_on_beta_interval`, `lemma_17_5_2_ratio_lower_of_uniform_correlation_on_self_Icc`, `lemma_17_5_2_upper_bound_of_high_temp_uniform_correlation_lower`, `lemma_17_5_2_sandwich_of_high_temp_uniform_correlation_lower`, `lemma_17_5_2_upper_bound_of_uniform_correlation_lower_on_self_Icc`, and `lemma_17_5_2_sandwich_of_uniform_correlation_lower_on_self_Icc` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma_17_5_2/FiniteHLSLipschitzPackage/RatioLower.lean`): derives the eventual interval-uniform finite ratio lower bound from uniform convergence `corr_n -> corr_infty`, a positive uniform lower bound `C ≤ corr_infty(β)`, denominator positivity, and a uniform denominator bound `h(β)^(2α) ≤ H`. The resulting witness `L = (C/2)/H` feeds directly into the high-temperature ratio-lower upper-bound and sandwich packages, and the inherited pseudo-mass equation premise is only interval-local `EventuallyEq`. The self-interval forms use `Icc β₁ β₂` itself as the high-temperature compact interval, deriving `0 < β₁` and `β₂ J 2d < 1` from the high-temperature inclusion via the shared endpoint helper now housed in the finite-HLS package. Callers can replace the raw finite ratio-lower premise by infinite-correlation and denominator bounds; the remaining substantive input is proving those interval bounds for the concrete pseudo-mass profile in the desired high-temperature regime. |

| §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 Simon-Lieb exp-form correlation bound (Step 119 plan Step 5.7g) | **Done (exp-form Simon-Lieb output)** | `simonLiebRate`, `simonLiebRate_nonneg`, `simonLiebRate_pos`, `betaJ_two_d_pow_eq_exp_neg_simonLiebRate_mul`, and `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_exp_neg_simonLiebRate_pow_of_dist_gt` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/SimonLiebDistanceDecay.lean`): expresses the existing Simon-Lieb peeling output `correlationInfinite ≤ (β·J·2d)^n` (PR via `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_betaJ_two_d_pow_of_dist_gt`) in exp form `correlationInfinite ≤ exp(-(simonLiebRate · n))` with `simonLiebRate β J d := -log(β·J·2d)`. Note: the exponent is `n = dist - 1` from peeling, not `dist`; converting to the `correlation ≤ exp(-(M·dist))` shape for direct use with Step 5.7e introduces a constant prefactor `exp(simonLiebRate) = 1/(β·J·2d)` (a known peeling artifact).
