| §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 HLS correlation pair-product capstone (Step 119 plan Step 5.6) | **Done (abstract bridge + HLS sum capstone)** | `PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge` structure and `tsum_correlationInfinite_pair_product_le_HLS_const` (`IsingModel/PseudoMass/HLSCorrelationCapstone.lean`, new file): abstracts the pseudo-mass-to-lattice-distance lower bound `M_inf · d(x,z) ≤ m⁻·r'` (together with the ferromagnetic assumption and per-pair active-range membership) as a structure, and uses it to derive the GJ p. 312 HLS-form sum bound `∑_z ⟨φ(x₀)φ(z)⟩·⟨φ(y₀)φ(z)⟩ ≤ K` for any `x₀, y₀ : Fin d → ℤ`, where `K = 4 · (max(1, (M_inf^α)⁻¹) · 2^α)² · K_HLS`. Hypotheses: `1 ≤ α`, `0 < r'`, `d < 2 * α`. Proof outline: (i) at `z = x₀` or `z = y₀`, `{x, x} = {x}` (Finset insert idempotent) and the singleton correlation vanishes by the h = 0 Z₂ symmetry on odd cardinality, so the pair product is 0; (ii) for `z ∉ {x₀, y₀}`, Step 5.1 (#3154) gives `corr ≤ 2/(1+(m⁻·r')^α)`, `bridge.bound` gives `m⁻·r' ≥ M_inf·d`, and monotonicity yields `corr ≤ 2/(1+(M_inf·d)^α)`; (iii) the pair-product sum is then bounded via #3170 (`discrete_hls_pseudoMass_two_pair_convolution_constant`). Summability of the LHS is reconstructed from the AM-GM bound `((1+d_x)^(-2α) + (1+d_y)^(-2α))/2`, paralleling `tsum_pseudoMass_pair_product_le_const_pow_M`. The abstract bridge can be supplied by the conditional family constructor when its inputs are already available, but the positive high-temperature route should use the direct Simon-Lieb trichotomy constructors with their adjacent/bound/active inputs. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe §17.5 p. 312. |

The capstone wrappers return the same HLS convolution witness, named `Lemma_17_5_2_UpperBound` predicate, and displayed sandwich for one constant `K`. For the future derivative route, the external inputs remain the active-range interval, the finite-derivative limit, and the lower validating exponential decay for the sandwich/capstone lower side. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 and Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311--312. |

| §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 derivative-limit provider criteria | **Done (conditional provider criteria; future derivative route)** | `DerivativeLimitProviderCriteria.lean` adds the Dini criteria `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_monotone_deriv_profiles` and `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_antitone_deriv_profiles`, proving `Lemma_17_5_2_DerivativeLimitProvider` from pointwise convergence to a continuous limiting derivative profile, finite derivative-profile continuity, and either stagewise monotonicity or antitonicity. The same module also adds compact-uniform-Cauchy criteria: `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_uniformCauchy_on_compacts`, `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_metricCauchy_on_compacts`, `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_uniformCauchy_on_Icc`, `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_metricCauchy_on_Icc`, and `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_metricCauchy_on_Icc_complete`. The provider extraction lemmas `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_tendstoUniformlyOn_Icc`, `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_uniformCauchySeqOn_Icc`, and `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_metricCauchy_on_Icc` expose the closed-interval uniform convergence and Cauchy consequences on the Cauchy/criteria side, keeping the core provider module free of the metric-Cauchy import. The legacy Cauchy criteria prove the provider from pointwise convergence plus uniform Cauchy control on every compact subset, or equivalently on every closed interval contained in the open high-temperature region. The complete closed-interval metric-Cauchy criterion constructs the pointwise derivative-profile limit by completeness of `ℝ`, so compact-interval metric Cauchy control alone supplies the provider. The convergence-rate criteria `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_summable_increments_eventually`, its all-stage corollary `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_summable_increments`, and `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_geometric_increments` discharge that closed-interval metric Cauchy control from a concrete bound on consecutive finite-volume stages: an eventual summable bound `c k` (`k ≥ N₀`) on the consecutive-stage derivative differences (proved by a telescoping/summable-tail argument with `dist_le_Ico_sum_of_dist_le` and partial-sum Cauchyness, raising the metric-Cauchy threshold to `max N N₀` so the finitely many uncontrolled head increments never enter), the all-stage `N₀ = 0` corollary, and its geometric specialization `M · ratio ^ k` (`0 ≤ ratio < 1`) matching cluster-expansion convergence rates. The eventual form matches the actual finite-volume structure, since for a fixed pair the derivative profiles vanish before the exhaustion stage contains the pair. For the future derivative route, this turns the derivative-limit task into concrete Dini-ready, compact-Cauchy, or summable-convergence-rate derivative-profile obligations. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 and Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311--312; Mathlib Dini theorem, uniform Cauchy convergence on sets, and the geometric-series summability criterion. |
| §17.5 | Lemma 17.5.2 finite derivative-profile continuity for the Dini provider | **Done (conditional finite-profile API; future derivative route)** | `DerivativeLimitProviderFiniteProfile.lean` proves the explicit finite derivative-profile formulas `lemma_17_5_2_finite_derivative_profile_eq_beta_edge_sum` and `lemma_17_5_2_finite_derivative_profile_eq_zero_of_not_subset`: after `{x,z}` is contained in the stage volume, the beta derivative is the finite Lebowitz edge sum over the induced graph; before that stage, the along-exhaustion correlation is identically zero and the derivative is zero. It also proves `lemma_17_5_2_finite_deriv_abs_le_high_temp_on_Icc_all_stages`, an all-stage closed-interval high-temperature absolute derivative bound: covered stages use the finite Lebowitz/susceptibility estimate, while uncovered stages use the zero formula, so the estimate no longer needs an eventual containment restriction at the finite-profile layer. The new limit-transfer theorems `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_profile_abs_le_high_temp_on_Icc` and `lemma_17_5_2_correlationInfinite_deriv_abs_le_high_temp_on_Icc` pass this all-stage finite bound through a derivative-limit provider to bound the limiting derivative profile and then the beta derivative of the infinite-volume two-point function. It then proves `lemma_17_5_2_finite_derivative_profile_continuous_beta`: for every exhaustion stage, the beta-derivative profile of the finite two-point function is continuous in beta. The new limit-continuity theorems `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_profile_continuousOn_high_temp` and `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_profile_continuousOn_Icc` use locally uniform/uniform convergence to pass this finite continuity to the provider witness on the open high-temperature region and on each closed subinterval. The infinite-volume theorem `lemma_17_5_2_correlationInfinite_deriv_continuousOn_high_temp` identifies that continuous provider witness with the beta derivative of the infinite-volume two-point function, proving continuity of the infinite beta derivative on the open high-temperature region. The regularity theorems `lemma_17_5_2_correlationInfinite_differentiableOn_high_temp` and `lemma_17_5_2_correlationInfinite_contDiffOn_one_high_temp` combine the provider-based derivative existence with this derivative continuity to show that the infinite-volume two-point function is differentiable and `C^1` in beta on the open high-temperature region. The strengthened criteria `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_{monotone,antitone}_deriv_profiles_finite_continuous` feed finite continuity automatically into the Dini provider criteria, leaving only the stagewise monotonicity/antitonicity, pointwise convergence, and limiting-profile continuity needed to build a provider via Dini. The new increment-structure theorems `lemma_17_5_2_finite_derivative_increment_eq_zero_of_not_subset` (the consecutive β-derivative increment vanishes before the pair-coverage stage, by exhaustion monotonicity and the zero formula) and `lemma_17_5_2_derivative_limit_provider_of_summable_increments_on_covered_stages` (a summable bound on the increments over covered stages supplies the provider, using `Exhaustion.exhaust` to discharge the eventual hypothesis without locating the onset index) localize the convergence-rate task to the covered stages. The increment magnitude is controlled uniformly by `lemma_17_5_2_finite_derivative_increment_abs_le_high_temp_on_Icc_all_stages` and its self-interval form `lemma_17_5_2_finite_derivative_increment_abs_le_high_temp_on_self_Icc`: on a closed high-temperature interval `|F_{k+1} - F_k| ≤ 2·(J·M² + 4dJ)` uniformly in the stage `k`, by the triangle inequality from the all-stage finite derivative bound. This uniform bound is not yet summable; sharpening it to a summable (geometric) bound from the finite-volume convergence rate is the future derivative-route input (former Issue #2931). PR #4899 migrated `lemma_17_5_2_finite_derivative_profile_eq_beta_edge_sum` and `lemma_17_5_2_finite_derivative_profile_eq_zero_of_not_subset` to the `correlationAlongExhaustion_family_eq_{of_subset, zero_of_not_subset}` family lemmas (proof-only; statements unchanged). Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 and Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.5 / §4.6 | Correlation increments along an exhaustion (convergence-rate baseline) | **Done (correlation increments nonneg + summable)** | `AmbientLattice/CorrelationInfinite/Increments.lean` proves `correlationAlongExhaustion_increment_nonneg` (the consecutive-stage correlation difference is nonnegative by GKS monotonicity) and `correlationAlongExhaustion_increment_summable` (the increment series is summable, with partial sums telescoping via `Finset.sum_range_sub` to `correlationAlongExhaustion ... n - correlationAlongExhaustion ... 0`, bounded above by `correlationInfinite - correlationAlongExhaustion ... 0`, closed by `summable_of_sum_range_le`). For correlations the increment series is automatically summable by monotone convergence; this frames the future derivative-route task (former Issue #2931) of obtaining a quantitative summable bound on the β-derivative increments used by the Lemma 17.5.2 derivative-limit provider. The same file records the convergence-tail structure `correlationInfinite_sub_correlationAlongExhaustion_nonneg` (the tail `c_∞ − c_n ≥ 0`), `_antitone` (the tail decreases as the volume grows, by GKS), and `tendsto_correlationInfinite_sub_correlationAlongExhaustion_zero` (the tail tends to `0`); the tail is the nonnegative antitone quantity whose decay rate is the future derivative-route convergence-rate input. The identity `correlationAlongExhaustion_increment_tsum_eq` records that the increment series sums (telescopically) to the full tail `∑' n, (c_{n+1} − c_n) = c_∞ − c_0`, and `correlationAlongExhaustion_tail_eq_tsum_increment_shift` expresses the stage-`n` tail as the increment tail-sum `c_∞ − c_n = ∑' k, (c_{k+n+1} − c_{k+n})` (so a quantitative bound on the increments from stage `n` controls the tail's decay). `correlationInfinite_sub_correlationAlongExhaustion_le_tsum_shift_of_increment_le` turns this into a quantitative bound: if the increments are dominated by a summable `g`, then `c_∞ − c_n ≤ ∑' k, g(k+n)` (the volume-convergence tail bounded by the increment-bound tail — the Phase A→C bridge of former Issue #2965). Its geometric specialization `correlationInfinite_sub_correlationAlongExhaustion_le_geometric` gives the explicit rate `c_∞ − c_n ≤ M·ratio^n/(1−ratio)` from `c_{k+1} − c_k ≤ M·ratio^k` (`0 ≤ ratio < 1`), via `tsum_geometric_of_lt_one`, and `correlationAlongExhaustion_sub_le_geometric` gives the corresponding geometric Cauchy rate for the finite-volume correlations themselves (`c_n − c_m ≤ M·ratio^m/(1−ratio)`). Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 and Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.5 / §17.8 | Unconditional high-temperature mass gap on ℤ^d | **Done (positive-rate `HasExponentialDecay` from an explicit high-temperature condition)** | `HighTempMassGap.lean` proves `hasExponentialDecay_latticeGraph_of_high_temp`: for `β, J > 0` and the explicit condition `H := βJ · 2 · (d · (2(r+1)+1)^d) < 1`, the ℤ^d Ising model at `h = 0` has `HasExponentialDecay d Λ ⟨J,0,β⟩` with the positive rate `-log(H)/(r+2)`. At `h = 0` the Ursell two-point function equals the correlation (`truncated2Infinite_h_zero`), which the explicit ball-boundary decay (`correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_explicit_pow_dist`) bounds by `H^{⌊dist/(r+2)⌋} ≤ (1/H)·exp(-(-log H/(r+2))·dist)` (witness `C = 1/H`). This is a positive mass gap holding **unconditionally** (no polynomial-decay hypothesis; the ball-boundary shell-contraction axiom `shellSup_contraction` is still used), connecting the ball-boundary spatial decay to the established `HasExponentialDecay` predicate used by the lower-bound machinery. Composing with `latticeMass_ge_of_HasExponentialDecay`, the explicit bounds `latticeMass_ge_explicit_of_high_temp` (`ENNReal.ofReal (-log(H)/(r+2)) ≤ latticeMass d Λ ⟨J,0,β⟩`) and `latticeMass_pos_of_high_temp_mass_gap` (`0 < latticeMass`) connect the mass gap to the central Lemma 17.5.2 quantity `latticeMass`, again unconditionally. `summable_truncated2Infinite_prod_of_high_temp` composes the mass gap with the Step 127 convolution summability to give, unconditionally at high temperature, the boundary-product summability `∑_z ⟨σ_xσ_z⟩^∞·⟨σ_yσ_z⟩^∞ < ∞`, and `tsum_truncated2Infinite_prod_decay_of_high_temp` strengthens this (via Step 127's quantitative `tsum_truncated2Infinite_prod_le`) to exponential decay of the convolution in `dist(x,y)`: `∑_z ⟨σ_xσ_z⟩^∞·⟨σ_yσ_z⟩^∞ ≤ C · exp(-(rate/2)·dist(x,y)/2)`, and `truncated2Infinite_prod_finset_sum_decay_of_high_temp` extends this bound to the partial sum over **any** finite vertex set `S` (uniformly in the choice of separating surface) — the boundary-sum ingredients for the finite-volume→infinite-volume convergence-rate coupling (former Issue #2965, Phase A/B). The **axiom-free** companion `hasExponentialDecay_latticeGraph_of_betaJ_two_d_lt_one` proves, for `β, J > 0`, `d ≥ 1`, and the simpler elementary condition `βJ·2d < 1`, both `0 < -log(βJ·2d)` and `HasExponentialDecay d (cubicExhaustion d) ⟨J,0,β⟩ (-log(βJ·2d))` — using only the iterated naive Simon–Lieb peeling decay `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_betaJ_two_d_pow_of_dist_gt` (`⟨σ_iσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ (βJ·2d)^{dist−1}`) with **no ball-boundary shell-contraction axiom**, via `(βJ·2d)^{dist−1} = (1/βJ·2d)·exp(log(βJ·2d)·dist)` (witness `C = 1/(βJ·2d)`). This is a weaker high-temperature hypothesis (no `(2(r+1)+1)^d` boundary factor) and axiom-free, at the cost of the cruder per-lattice-step base `βJ·2d` rather than the per-shell contraction factor. Composing with `latticeMass_ge_of_HasExponentialDecay`, `latticeMass_ge_of_betaJ_two_d_lt_one` gives the axiom-free lower bound `ENNReal.ofReal (-log(βJ·2d)) ≤ latticeMass d (cubicExhaustion d) ⟨J,0,β⟩`, and `latticeMass_pos_of_betaJ_two_d_lt_one` the axiom-free positive mass gap `0 < latticeMass` — both under the elementary `βJ·2d < 1`, with no shell-contraction axiom. `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_susceptibility_summable_betaJ_two_d` likewise gives the axiom-free **finite susceptibility** `χ = ∑_y ⟨σ_0σ_y⟩^∞ < ∞` under `βJ·2d < 1`: the decay `(βJ·2d)^{dist−1}` is dominated by the summable majorant `(βJ·2d)^{⌊dist/2⌋}` (`summable_pow_div_latticeDistance d 0`, via `⌊dist/2⌋ ≤ dist−1`), the `y=0` term `≤ 1`. By translation invariance `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_susceptibility_summable_betaJ_two_d_basepoint` gives the same axiom-free finite susceptibility from any basepoint `x` (basepoint-independent). `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_uniform_decay_of_betaJ_two_d_lt_one` records the corresponding axiom-free uniform clustering: for every `ε > 0` there is `R` with `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ ε` whenever `dist(i,j) ≥ R` (via `exists_pow_lt_of_lt_one` on `(βJ·2d)^{dist−1}`). Its topological form `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_tendsto_cofinite_zero_of_betaJ_two_d_lt_one` states that `y ↦ ⟨σ_0σ_y⟩^∞` tends to `0` along the cofinite filter (the `C₀` clustering property), via the finiteness of lattice balls `latticeDistance_le_finite`. Finally `summable_truncated2Infinite_prod_of_betaJ_two_d_lt_one` gives the axiom-free **boundary two-point convolution summability** `∑_z ⟨σ_xσ_z⟩^∞·⟨σ_yσ_z⟩^∞ < ∞` (the GJ §17.5 boundary-sum ingredient for the finite→infinite-volume convergence-rate coupling, former Issue #2965 Phase B), composing the axiom-free decay with the generic `summable_truncated2Infinite_prod_of_hasExponentialDecay`. The quantitative form `tsum_truncated2Infinite_prod_decay_of_betaJ_two_d_lt_one` strengthens this to exponential decay of the convolution in `dist(x,y)`: `∑_z ⟨σ_xσ_z⟩^∞·⟨σ_yσ_z⟩^∞ ≤ C·exp(-(rate/2)·dist(x,y)/2)` with `rate = -log(βJ·2d)`, via the Step 127 bound `tsum_truncated2Infinite_prod_le` — the axiom-free Phase-B convolution decay. The partial-sum form `truncated2Infinite_prod_finset_sum_decay_of_betaJ_two_d_lt_one` extends this to the convolution over **any** finite vertex set `S` (uniformly in the separating surface): `∑_{b∈S} ⟨σ_xσ_b⟩^∞·⟨σ_yσ_b⟩^∞ ≤ C·exp(-(rate/2)·dist(x,y)/2)`, via `Summable.sum_le_tsum`. This completes the axiom-free mirror of the shell-based high-temperature package (decay → mass gap → susceptibility → clustering → boundary convolution), all under the elementary `βJ·2d < 1`. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5, §17.8 Theorem 17.8.1, pp. 311--318. |
| §17.5 | Simon--Lieb one-step decay on the integer lattice (future derivative-route support) | **Done (spatial decay and convergence-rate infrastructure)** | `SimonLiebDistanceDecay.lean` proves `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_of_neighbors_le`: for a non-adjacent pair `i ≠ j` on `ℤ^d`, if every neighbour `k` of `i` satisfies `⟨σ_kσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ C` (`C ≥ 0`), then `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ βJ · 2d · C`, by the Simon--Lieb peeling inequality `correlationInfinite_simon_lieb_latticeGraph` and the degree bound `latticeGraph_degree_le` (degree `≤ 2d`). These are the single-vertex building blocks. The corollary `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_betaJ_two_d_of_not_adj` applies the one-step bound (under `0 ≤ βJ`) with the universal `≤ 1` bound to give `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ βJ·2d` for every non-adjacent pair — a contraction factor `< 1` in the strict high-temperature regime `0 ≤ βJ·2d < 1`. The second iterate `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_betaJ_two_d_sq_of_dist_ge_three` shows naive peeling *does* iterate once the separation is large enough: for `dist(i,j) ≥ 3` every neighbour `k` of `i` still satisfies `dist(k,j) ≥ dist(i,j) − 1 ≥ 2`, hence is non-adjacent to `j` and obeys `⟨σ_kσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ βJ·2d`, so `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ (βJ·2d)²` (squaring the contraction factor). Iterating fully, `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_betaJ_two_d_pow_of_dist_gt` gives `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ (βJ·2d)^n` whenever `dist(i,j) ≥ n + 1`, i.e. `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ (βJ·2d)^{dist(i,j) − 1}` — prefactor-free geometric decay with explicit base `βJ·2d` (no contraction-factor abstraction and no ball-boundary shell axiom), by induction on `n` (the `n = 0` base bounds by `1`; each step peels via `_le_of_neighbors_le` with `C = (βJ·2d)^n`). The same reverse-triangle step continues while the separation from `j` stays `≥ 2`; the obstruction is the distance-`2` base case (a neighbour can be *adjacent* to `j`, bounded only by `1`, with no `≤ βJ·2d` bound for adjacent pairs), which caps the gain near `j`. A clean prefactor-free exponential distance decay (one contraction per shell, with no such near-`j` defect) instead uses the separating-surface (ball-boundary) Simon--Lieb argument `ball_boundary_simon_lieb` applied to cubic shells. That spatial decay is in fact already available via the shell iteration `shellSup_iterated_bound` (`TheoremEtaLe1/Contraction/Iterated.lean`); its pointwise form `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_contractionFactor_pow_dist` gives `⟨σ_0σ_y⟩^∞ ≤ (contractionFactor d Λ p r)^{dist(0,y)/(r+2)}` for every `y ≠ 0` (prefactor-free, contractionFactor `< 1`), and the translation-invariant form `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_contractionFactor_pow_dist_pair` extends it to every distinct pair `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ (contractionFactor)^{dist(i,j)/(r+2)}`. The consequence `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_uniform_decay_of_contractionFactor_lt_one` records the resulting uniform clustering: for every `ε > 0` there is `R` with `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ ε` whenever `dist(i,j) ≥ R`. Its topological form `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_tendsto_cofinite_zero` states that `y ↦ ⟨σ_0σ_y⟩^∞` tends to `0` along the cofinite filter (the `C₀`/clustering property), via the finiteness of lattice balls `latticeDistance_le_finite`. The summability form `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_susceptibility_summable` records that, when `0 < contractionFactor < 1`, the correlation kernel is summable over `ℤ^d` (finite magnetic susceptibility `χ = ∑_y ⟨σ_0σ_y⟩^∞`): the per-pair decay is dominated by `(1/contractionFactor)·exp(-m·dist)` with `m = -log(contractionFactor)/(r+2) > 0`, summable by `summable_exp_neg_dist`. By translation invariance `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_susceptibility_summable_basepoint` gives the same finiteness from any basepoint `x` (the susceptibility is basepoint-independent). The cardinality bound `latticeBallBoundaryEdges_card_le` (the ball-boundary separating edge set has at most the edge count of the cube `cubicBox d (r+1)`, being a filtered image of its edge finset) bounds the size of the Simon--Lieb separating surface, and `contractionFactor_le_card` bounds the contraction factor itself: `contractionFactor d Λ p r ≤ βJ · 2 · |latticeBallBoundaryEdges d r|` (each boundary-edge summand `⟨σ_0σ_k⟩^∞ + ⟨σ_0σ_l⟩^∞ ≤ 2`). Chaining these with the cube edge count and cardinality, `contractionFactor_le_high_temp_const` gives the explicit volume-independent bound `contractionFactor d Λ p r ≤ βJ · 2 · (d · (2(r+1)+1)^d)`, and `contractionFactor_lt_one_of_high_temp` concludes `contractionFactor < 1` **unconditionally** when `βJ · 2 · (d · (2(r+1)+1)^d) < 1` (no polynomial-decay hypothesis), discharging the spatial-decay/susceptibility layer's hypothesis in an explicit strong-high-temperature regime. Composing this with the clustering lemmas, `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_uniform_decay_of_high_temp` and `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_tendsto_cofinite_zero_of_high_temp` give unconditional uniform large-distance decay and cofinite vanishing of the correlation kernel directly from the explicit high-temperature condition (no polynomial-decay hypothesis). `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_le_explicit_pow_dist` gives the fully explicit prefactor-free geometric decay `⟨σ_iσ_j⟩^∞ ≤ H^{dist(i,j)/(r+2)}` with base `H = βJ · 2 · (d · (2(r+1)+1)^d) < 1` (no contraction factor in the statement and no polynomial-decay hypothesis; the ball-boundary shell-contraction axiom `shellSup_contraction` is still used), via `cf ≤ H` and base monotonicity `pow_le_pow_left₀`. Consequently `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_susceptibility_summable_high_temp` gives the **unconditional finite susceptibility** at strong high temperature (under `0 < βJ` and `H < 1`): the kernel is summable, with no polynomial-decay hypothesis and no contraction-factor positivity — the explicit decay dominates it by the summable `H^{dist/(r+2)}` (`summable_pow_div_latticeDistance`, the reusable `0 < c < 1` floor-power lattice-summability core). By translation invariance `correlationInfinite_latticeGraph_susceptibility_summable_high_temp_basepoint` gives the same unconditional finiteness from any basepoint. For the future derivative-convergence route, the former Phase-3a/3b′ gap is the distinct *volume-convergence* rate of the finite-volume β-derivative increments, which the boundary-effect estimate relates to this spatial decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Lemma 17.5.2 pp. 311--312; Simon 1980, Comm. Math. Phys. 77, 111--126; Lieb 1980, Comm. Math. Phys. 77, 127--135. |
| §17.5 / §4.6 | Cubic-exhaustion boundary-distance geometry (convergence-rate foundation) | **Done (fresh-vertex sup-norm and ℓ¹-distance lower bounds)** | `CubicExhaustion.lean` proves `exists_coord_natAbs_eq_of_mem_cubicBox_succ_not_mem` (a vertex in the stage-`n+1` cube but not the stage-`n` cube has a coordinate of absolute value exactly `n+1`, i.e. it sits on the sup-norm sphere of radius `n+1`) and `latticeDistance_ge_of_mem_cubicBox_succ_not_mem` (for a reference point `p` in the radius-`R` cube and a fresh stage-`n+1` vertex `w` with `R ≤ n`, the ℓ¹ lattice distance satisfies `n + 1 - R ≤ latticeDistance d p w`). The companion `latticeDistance_ge_of_adj_mem_cubicBox_succ_not_mem` extends this to neighbours of fresh vertices: a vertex adjacent to a fresh stage-`n+1` vertex is still at ℓ¹-distance at least `n - R` from the radius-`R` reference point (adjacency is unit ℓ¹-distance, so a neighbour loses at most one unit), the form needed when the increment is expanded over the fresh boundary edges. These pin fresh exhaustion vertices (and their incident edges) to receding boundary spheres, the geometric foundation for the boundary-distance exponential-decay argument behind the finite-volume β-derivative increment convergence rate (former Issue #2931, future route). The cardinality side is also in place: `card_cubicBox` (`(cubicBox d n).card = (2n+1)^d`), `card_cubicBox_sdiff_succ_le` (the fresh boundary set has at most `(2n+3)^d` vertices), and `summable_cubicBox_boundary_card_mul_geometric` (`n ↦ (2n+3)^d · ratio^n` is summable for `0 ≤ ratio < 1`). Together these give the summability skeleton — polynomial boundary growth against geometric decay — for the eventual increment bound. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.5 Theorem 17.5.1 and Lemma 17.5.2, pp. 311--312; §4.6, p. 67. |

Explicit named aliases of `truncated2_nonneg`/`truncated2Infinite_nonneg` (GKS-II) and `cor_4_3_3`/`truncated4Infinite_nonpos_h_zero` (Lebowitz at `h = 0`). (PR #636.) |
| §17.7 | Critical exponents: `ν ≥ ½` and `γ ≥ 1` (Thm 17.7.1, pp. 314–315) — scope determination | **Out of scope (depend on §17.4 continuum machinery)** | GJ Theorem 17.7.1 claims that each critical exponent (ν, γ, η, ζ) is ≥ its Gaussian value (ν_cl = ½, γ_cl = 1, η_cl = 0, ζ_cl = 0). The lattice-Ising analogs `eta_nonneg_finite_vol` and `zeta_nonneg_finite_vol` above (derived from GKS-II and Lebowitz Cor 4.3.3) are in-scope and formalized. However, GJ's proofs of `γ ≥ 1` and `ν ≥ ½` are explicitly routed through §17.4 in the book text (GJ pp. 314–315): "`γ ≥ 1` follows from `0 ≤ dχ/dσ ≤ 1`, established in **Section 17.4**" and "`ν ≥ ½` via the bound `dm²/dσ ≤ Z` (**Section 17.4**)", where `Z` is the field-strength renormalization constant from the Källén–Lehmann spectral theory (see group 2d). The book provides no alternative elementary/lattice derivation of `γ ≥ 1` or `ν ≥ ½` that avoids §17.4's continuum machinery. Since §17.4 is out-of-scope, both `ν ≥ ½` and `γ ≥ 1` are out-of-scope by design (not a formalization gap, but a boundary of the project's scope). The file `IsingModel/PhaseTransition/CriticalGrowth.lean` lines 84–85 self-documents this: "`ν ≥ ½`: from correlation length bounds (requires spectral theory)" and "`γ ≥ 1`: from susceptibility bounds (χ monotone, requires more)". GJ pp. 314–315. Issue #4405 group 2e. |
| §17.8 | Coupling derivative formula (Step 135) | **Done** | `scaledBoltzmannWeight G E₀ p s σ`: Boltzmann weight with E₀-bonds scaled by s (s=0: no E₀; s=1: full). `hasDerivAt_scaledBoltzmannWeight`: `d/ds w_s(σ) = β·J·(Σ_{E₀}σₑ)·w_s(σ)`. `hasDerivAt_scaledPartitionFunction`, `hasDerivAt_scaledCorrelation`: `d/ds ⟨σ^A⟩_s = β·J·Σ_{e=(u,v)∈E₀}(⟨σ^{AΔ{u,v}}⟩_s − ⟨σ^A⟩_s·⟨σ^{u,v}⟩_s)`. Reference: GJ §17.8 pp. 316–318. (PR #952 Step 135, Issue #951.) |
| §17.8 | Ball-boundary Simon-Lieb inequality (Step 136) | **Done** | GKS-I for scaled model (`scaledCorrelation_nonneg`), GKS-II for scaled model (`scaledCorrelation_gks_second`, duplicate-variable trick), monotonicity in s (`scaledCorrelation_monotoneOn`). `ball_boundary_simon_lieb`: `⟨σ_r σ_s⟩ ≤ β·J·Σ_{(k,l)∈E₀}[⟨σ_r σ_k⟩·⟨σ_s σ_l⟩ + ⟨σ_r σ_l⟩·⟨σ_s σ_k⟩ + ⟨σ_r σ_s⟩·⟨σ_k σ_l⟩]`. Tight form `ball_boundary_simon_lieb_tight` (no extra term, uses the proven `cor_4_3_3_scaled` theorem — axiom discharged in PR #3912). `scaledCorrelation_odd_vanish` (h=0 → odd-card vanish for scaled model). The mean-value step of `ball_boundary_simon_lieb` is extracted separately as `scaledCorrelation_one_sub_zero_le_derivBound` (without the disconnection/vanishing hypothesis): `scaledCorrelation G E₀ p 1 {r,s} − scaledCorrelation G E₀ p 0 {r,s} ≤ derivBound G E₀ p r s`, bounding the *difference* between two finite-volume systems differing only on boundary edges `E₀` — the finite-volume coupling step toward the per-stage correlation increment bound for the volume-convergence rate (former Issue #2965, Phase A). The `s = 0` system is identified concretely with the **bond-deleted** model: `scaledBoltzmannWeight_zero`, `scaledPartitionFunction_zero`, `scaledGibbsExpectation_zero`, and `scaledCorrelation_zero` (all in `CouplingDerivative.lean`) prove that at `s = 0` the scaling factor `exp(−β·J·∑_{e∈E₀} σ_e)` exactly cancels the `E₀` edges' Hamiltonian contribution (for `E₀ ⊆ G.edgeFinset`), so `scaledCorrelation G E₀ p 0 A = correlation (G.deleteEdges ↑E₀) p A` — the free-boundary model on the sub-graph with the `E₀` bonds removed (via `SimpleGraph.edgeFinset_deleteEdges` and `Finset.sum_sdiff`). This makes the ball-boundary disconnection (`scaledCorrelation_at_zero_of_sep`) concrete and is the conceptual basis for the finite-volume-stage coupling toward the volume-convergence rate. Combining the difference bound with the bond-deleted identification, `correlation_sub_deleteEdges_le_derivBound` (`WeakBound.lean`) gives the increment entirely in standard-correlation terms: `correlation G p {r,s} − correlation (G.deleteEdges ↑E₀) p {r,s} ≤ derivBound G E₀ p r s` (adding the bonds `E₀` raises `⟨σ_r σ_s⟩` by at most `derivBound`). `correlation_deleteEdges_le` records the matching GKS bond-monotonicity (deleting bonds cannot increase a correlation), so the increment is nonnegative, and `derivBound_le_card` (`derivBound ≤ β·J·(3·|E₀|)`, each correlation product `≤ 1`) yields the coarse a-priori cardinality bound `correlation_sub_deleteEdges_le_card`: `correlation G p {r,s} − correlation (G.deleteEdges ↑E₀) p {r,s} ≤ β·J·(3·|E₀|)`. For the boundary-sum *decay* step (former Issue #2965, Phase A→B link), `derivBound_le_of_correlation_le` records the monotonicity of `derivBound` under correlation upper bounds: for a symmetric nonnegative `c : ι → ι → ℝ` with `correlation G p {a,b} ≤ c a b`, `derivBound G E₀ p r s ≤ β·J·∑_{⟨k,l⟩∈E₀}[c r k·c s l + c r l·c s k + c r s·c k l]` (each summand a sum of nonnegative correlation products, monotone under the pointwise bound). This separates the `derivBound` algebra from the eventual substitution `c a b =` an infinite-volume decay bound (via finite-volume ≤ infinite-volume monotonicity), so the divergent-prefactor-sensitive decay analysis can be done downstream without re-touching the edge sum. Reference: GJ §17.8 pp. 316–318. (PRs #953, #954 Steps 136–137, Issue #951; #2972, #2973, #2974 former Issue #2965.) |
| §17.8 | **Infinite-volume tight ball-boundary Simon–Lieb inequality** (`ball_boundary_tight_infinite`, formerly axiom) | **Done (axiom discharged; hypotheses corrected)** | `BallBoundaryInfinite.lean`: `ball_boundary_tight_infinite` is now a **theorem** — for ferromagnetic `h = 0`, `1 ≤ r`, and `r + 1 < latticeDistance d 0 x`, `⟨σ₀σ_x⟩_∞ ≤ βJ·∑_{(k,l)∈∂B_r}[⟨σ₀σ_k⟩_∞⟨σ_lσ_x⟩_∞ + ⟨σ₀σ_l⟩_∞⟨σ_kσ_x⟩_∞]`. Helpers: `mem_cubicBox_of_latticeDistance_origin_le`, `latticeBallBoundary_confine`, `mem_latticeBallBoundaryEdges`. **Correctness finding**: the original axiom (stated for all `r` with `r < dist`) is false for `r = 0` (origin on every boundary edge; `{0,0}→⟨σ₀⟩=0`) and for `dist = r+1` (sink on a boundary edge); the corrected `1 ≤ r ∧ r+1 < dist` is the genuine validity range (the distance part is supplied by `shellSup_contraction`'s shell at distance `≥ r+2`; `1 ≤ r` must additionally be threaded into `shellSup_contraction` when it is discharged). Reference: GJ §17.8 (17.8.4)–(17.8.5), pp. 316–318. |
